\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{log_sum}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\delta}{2^{m+3}}\cdot 2^{m+1}\cdot 2\leq\frac{\delta}{2}.

(71)

Upper bound on
\delta^{\star}

The probability lower bound in
Theorem

1
is

1\!-\!\delta^{\prime}\!-\!\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2^{j-1}}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m-j}\lfloor{n/2^{m}}\rfloor}\delta_{i}
, which is non-negative only if

\textstyle 1\geq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2^{j-1}}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m-j}\lfloor{n/2^{m}}\rfloor}\delta_{i}\geq\frac{2^{m}}{2}\lfloor{\frac{n}{2^{m}}}\rfloor\delta^{\star},

(72)

which holds only if
\delta^{\star}\leq\frac{2}{2^{m}\lfloor{n/2^{m}}\rfloor}\leq\frac{6m}{2^{m}}
since
m\in[1,\log_{2}n]
. The claim follows.

Appendix G
Proof of
Corollary

1
: MMD rates for kernel thinning

Repeating arguments similar to those deriving
Eq.

355
in
Appendix

N
, we find that for the advertised choices of
m
and for any fixed
\delta
such that
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{2}
and
\log(1/\delta^{\star})=\mathcal{O}(\log(n/\delta))
, the RHS of the bound
Eq.

9
on MMD from
Theorem

1
can be simplified as follows:

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}})

(73)

\textstyle\leq c\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(c^{\prime}\frac{\max(R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}})^{2}}{d}}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}d^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n/\delta)}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log(\frac{8}{\delta})+\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2+\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}+R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}})}{\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}}}}\right)}}\right]},

(74)

\textstyle=c_{\delta,d}\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max(R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}})}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\sqrt{\log(1+\max(R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}))+\log(1+\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}{\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}})},

(75)

for some universal constants
c,c^{\prime}
where to simplify the expressions, we have used the fact that
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}\leq R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}

Eq.

7
.
Noting that
Eq.

75
holds with probability at least
1-\delta
,
Corollary

1
now follows from plugging the assumed growth rate bounds into the estimate
Eq.

75
, and treating
\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}
and
\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}{\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}}
as some constant while
n
grows.

Appendix H
Proof of
Theorem

2
: MMD guarantee for square-root
L^{\infty}
approximations

Our proof will use the following two lemmas proved in
Sections

H.1
and
H.2
respectively.

Lemma 7

(Square-root representation of MMD)
.

For
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
satisfying
1
with square-root kernel
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\in{L}^{2,\infty}

we have, for any distributions
\mu
and
\nu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mu,\nu)=\sup_{g\in{L}^{2}:\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int g(y)(\mu{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(y)-\nu{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(y))dy}}\right|.

(76)

Lemma 8

(
L^{\infty}
bound on
{L}^{2}
kernel error)
.

Consider any kernel
\mathbf{k}\in{L}^{2,\infty}
satisfying
1
, distributions
\mu,\nu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and function
g\in{L}^{2}
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
.
For any
r,a,b\geq 0
with
a+b=1
,

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int g(y)(\mu\mathbf{k}(y)-\nu\mathbf{k}(y))dy}}\right|

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mu\mathbf{k}-\nu\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\mathrm{Vol}^{\frac{1}{2}}(r)+2\tau_{\mathbf{k}}(ar)+2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{L^{2,\infty}}\max\{\tau_{\mu}(br),\tau_{\nu}(br)\},

(77)

where
\mathrm{Vol}(r)\triangleq\pi^{d}/\Gamma(d/2+1)r^{d}
denotes the volume of the Euclidean ball
\mathcal{B}(0;r)
.

We first note that, by the square-root kernel definition (
Definition

5
),
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,\cdot)}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}=\sqrt{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,x)}
for each
x\in\real^{d}
.
Since
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
is bounded, we therefore have
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\in{L}^{2,\infty}
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{L^{2,\infty}}=\sqrt{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}
.
The result now follows by invoking
Lemmas

7
and
8
with
\mathbf{k}={\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
.

H.1
Proof of
Lemma

7
: Square-root representation of MMD

Let
\mathcal{H}_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}
represent the RKHS of
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
.
By
Saitoh 1999

and the definition (
4
) of
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
, for any
f\in\mathcal{H}_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}
, there exists
a function
g\in{L}^{2}
such that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f}}}\right\|_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad f(x)=\int g(y){\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)dy\quad\text{for all}\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(78)

and, for any
g\in{L}^{2}
, there exists an
f\in\mathcal{H}_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}

such that
Eq.

78
holds.
Note that the integral in
Eq.

78
is well defined for each
x
since
g\in{L}^{2}
and
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\in{L}^{2,\infty}
.
Hence, we have

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mu,\nu)

\textstyle=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}:\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f}}}\right\|_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}\leq 1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mu f-\nu f}}\right|

(79)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\sup_{g\in{L}^{2}:\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{{L}^{2}}\leq 1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int\int g(y){\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(y,x)dyd\mu(x)-\int\int g(y){\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(y,x)dyd\nu(x)}}\right|

(80)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}\sup_{g\in{L}^{2}:\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{{L}^{2}}\leq 1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int g(y)\mu{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(y)dy-\int g(y)\nu{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(y)dy}}\right|.

(81)

where step (i) follows from
Eq.

78
, and we can swap the order of integration to obtain step (ii) using Fubini’s
theorem along with the following fact justified by Hölder’s inequality:

\textstyle\int\int\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|g(y){\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(y,x)}}\right|dyd\tilde{\mu}(x)\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{{L}^{2,\infty}}\int d\tilde{\mu}(x)<\infty\ \text{for any distribution.}\ \tilde{\mu}

(82)

H.2
Proof of
Lemma

8
:
L^{\infty}
bound on
{L}^{2}
kernel error

Fix any
r\geq 0
, introduce the shorthand
\mathcal{B}(r)=\mathcal{B}(0;r)
, and define the restrictions

\textstyle g_{r}(x)=g(x)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}(r)}(x),\quad\mathbf{k}_{r}(x,z)\triangleq\mathbf{k}(x,z)\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}(r)}(z),\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbf{k}^{(c)}_{r}\triangleq\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}_{r},

(83)

so that

\mu\mathbf{k}=\mu\mathbf{k}_{r}+\mu\mathbf{k}^{(c)}_{r}.

We first note that, by Cauchy-Schwarz,
g_{r}\in{L}^{1}\cap{L}^{2}
with

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g_{r}}}}\right\|_{{L}^{1}}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g_{r}}}}\right\|_{{L}^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\textrm{Vol}(r)}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{{L}^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\textrm{Vol}(r)}\leq\sqrt{\textrm{Vol}(r)}

(84)

and that, exactly as in
Eq.

82
,

\int\int\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|g(y)\mathbf{k}(y,x)}}\right|dyd\tilde{\mu}(x)<\infty
for any distribution
\tilde{\mu}
so that each of the integrals to follow is well defined.
We now apply the triangle inequality and Hölder’s inequality to obtain

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int g(y)(\mu\mathbf{k}(y)-\nu\mathbf{k}(y))dy}}\right|

\textstyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int g(y)(\mu\mathbf{k}_{r}(y)-\nu\mathbf{k}_{r}(y))dy+\int g(y)(\mu\mathbf{k}^{(c)}_{r}(y)-\nu\mathbf{k}^{(c)}_{r}(y))dy}}\right|

(85)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int g_{r}(y)(\mu\mathbf{k}_{r}(y)-\nu\mathbf{k}_{r}(y))dy}}\right|+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq r}g(y)(\mu\mathbf{k}^{(c)}_{r}(y)-\nu\mathbf{k}^{(c)}_{r}(y))dy}}\right|

(86)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g_{r}}}}\right\|_{{L}^{1}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mu\mathbf{k}\!-\!\nu\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\!+\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq r}g(y)(\mu\mathbf{k}(y)\!-\!\nu\mathbf{k}(y))dy}}\right|.

(87)

Next, we bound the second term in
Eq.

87
.
For any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq r
and scalars

a,b\in[0,1]
such that
a+b=1
,
either
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq ar
or
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x}}}\right\|_{2}\geq br
.
Hence,

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq r}g(y)(\mu\mathbf{k}(y)-\nu\mathbf{k}(y))dy}}\right|

\textstyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq r}g(y)\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{k}(x,y)(d\mu(x)-d\nu(x))dy}}\right|

(88)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq r}\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq ar}g(y)\mathbf{k}(x,y)(d\mu(x)-d\nu(x))dy}}\right|

(89)

\textstyle\qquad+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq r}\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x}}}\right\|_{2}\geq br}g(y)\mathbf{k}(x,y)(d\mu(x)-d\nu(x))dy}}\right|

(90)

\textstyle=:T_{1}+T_{2}.

(91)

Note that both
T_{1},T_{2}<\infty
since
g\in{L}^{2}
,
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\in{L}^{2,\infty}
and
\mu,\nu
are probability measures.
We now bound the terms
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
separately in
Eqs.

92d
and
92g

below. These bounds, together with the estimates
Eqs.

87
and
84
,
yield our claim.

Bounding
T_{1}

Substituting
x-y=z
, we have

\textstyle T_{1}

\textstyle\leq\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-z}}}\right\|_{2}\geq r}\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}\geq ar}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|g(x-z)\mathbf{k}(x,x-z)}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|d\mu(x)-d\nu(x)dz}}\right|

(92a)

\textstyle\leq\int\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}\geq ar}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|g(x-z)\mathbf{k}(x,x-z)}}\right|dz\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|d\mu(x)-d\nu(x)}}\right|

(92b)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\int\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g(x-\cdot)}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\sup_{x^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}\geq ar}\mathbf{k}^{2}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime}-z)dz}}\right)^{1/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|d\mu(x)-d\nu(x)}}\right|

(92c)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}\int\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\tau_{\mathbf{k}}(ar)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|d\mu(x)-d\nu(x)}}\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\tau_{\mathbf{k}}(ar),

(92d)

where step (i) follows from Cauchy-Schwarz, step (ii) from
the definition (
3
) of
\tau_{\mathbf{k}}
, and step (iii)
from the fact
\int\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|d\mu(x)-d\nu(x)}}\right|\leq 2
.

Bounding
T_{2}

We have

\textstyle T_{2}

\textstyle\leq\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x}}}\right\|_{2}\geq br}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq r}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|g(y)\mathbf{k}(x,y)}}\right|dy}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|d\mu(x)-d\nu(x)}}\right|

(92e)

\textstyle\leq\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x}}}\right\|_{2}\geq br}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\sup_{x^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}(x^{\prime},\cdot)}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|d\mu(x)-d\nu(x)}}\right|

(92f)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iv)}}{{\leq}}2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{g}}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{L^{2,\infty}}\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\tau_{\mu}(br),\tau_{\nu}(br)}}\right\}.

(92g)

where step (iv) follows from the definitions
3
and
26
of
(\tau_{\mu},\tau_{\nu})
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{L^{2,\infty}}
.

Appendix I
Proof of
Corollary

2
: MMD error from square-root
L^{\infty}
error

Let
e_{d}^{\prime}\triangleq 2^{\frac{d}{2}}e_{d}
for
e_{d}
defined in
Theorem

2
. Notice that the bound
Eq.

10
for the choice of
a=b=\frac{1}{2}
can be rewritten as

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\leq\inf_{r}(e_{d}^{\prime}r^{\frac{d}{2}}\varepsilon+2\widetilde{\tau}(r)).

(93)

Then the claims of
Corollary

2
follow by optimizing the RHS of
Eq.

93
over the choice of
r
depending on the tail decay of
\widetilde{\tau}
. Throughout the proofs
c,c^{\prime},\rho
denote the (exactly same) constants underlying the assumed tail decay of
\widetilde{\tau}
.

Proof for
Compact
part

Choosing
r\downarrow c^{\prime}
, we obtain that

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\leq\inf_{r}(e_{d}^{\prime}r^{\frac{d}{2}}\varepsilon+2c\mathbb{I}(r\leq c^{\prime}))\leq\varepsilon\cdot e_{d}^{\prime}(c^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}}.

(94)

Proof for
SubGauss
part

Choosing
r=\sqrt{\frac{1}{c^{\prime}}\log(1\vee\frac{8cc^{\prime}}{de_{d}^{\prime}\varepsilon})}
, we obtain that

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\leq\inf_{r}\big(e_{d}^{\prime}r^{\frac{d}{2}}\varepsilon+2ce^{-c^{\prime}r^{2}}\big)\leq\varepsilon\cdot e_{d}^{\prime}\big[\big(\frac{\log(1\vee\frac{8cc^{\prime}}{de_{d}^{\prime}\varepsilon})}{c^{\prime}}\big)^{\frac{d}{4}}+\frac{d}{4c^{\prime}}\big].

(95)

Proof for
SubExp
part

Choosing
r=\frac{1}{c^{\prime}}\log(1\vee\frac{4cc^{\prime}}{de_{d}^{\prime}\varepsilon})
, we obtain that

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\leq\inf_{r}\big(e_{d}^{\prime}r^{\frac{d}{2}}\varepsilon+2ce^{-c^{\prime}r}\big)\leq\varepsilon\cdot e_{d}^{\prime}\big[\big(\frac{\log(1\vee\frac{4cc^{\prime}}{de_{d}^{\prime}\varepsilon})}{c^{\prime}}\big)^{\frac{d}{2}}+\frac{d}{2c^{\prime}}\big].

(96)

Proof for
\textsc{HeavyTail}(\rho)
part

Choosing
r=(\frac{4c\rho}{de_{d}^{\prime}\varepsilon})^{\frac{2}{d+2\rho}}
, we obtain that

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\leq\inf_{r}\big(e_{d}^{\prime}r^{\frac{d}{2}}\varepsilon+2cr^{-\rho}\big)\leq(\varepsilon\cdot e_{d}^{\prime})^{\frac{2\rho}{d+2\rho}}\cdot(\frac{4c\rho}{d})^{\frac{d}{d+2\rho}}(1+\frac{2\rho}{d}).

(97)

Appendix J
Proof of
Theorem

3
: Self-balancing Hilbert walk properties

We prove each property from
Theorem

3
one by one.

J.1
Property
(i)
: Functional sub-Gaussianity

We prove the functional sub-Gaussianity claim
Eq.

13

by induction on the iteration
i\in\{0,\dots,n\}
.
Our proof uses
the following lemma proved in
Section

J.7
, which supplies a convenient decomposition for the self-balancing Hilbert walk iterates.

Lemma 9

(Alternate representation of
\psi_{i}
)
.

For each
i\in[n]
, the iterate
\psi_{i}
of the self-balancing Hilbert walk (
Algorithm

5
) satisfies

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i-1},u-f_{i}{\textstyle\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}+\varepsilon_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\quad\text{for all}\quad u\in\mathcal{H}

(98)

for the random variable
\varepsilon_{i}\triangleq\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\alpha_{i}}}\right|\leq\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right](\eta_{i}+\alpha_{i}/\mathfrak{a}_{i})
which satisfies

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}|\psi_{i-1}]=0,\quad\varepsilon_{i}\in[-2,2],\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}[e^{t\varepsilon_{i}}|\psi_{i-1}]\leq e^{t^{2}/2}\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

(99)

Now we proceed with our induction argument.

Base case

For
i=1
, noting that
\psi_{0}=0
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{1},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right)]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:temprv_defn}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{1},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right)|]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:temprv_constraints}}}{{\leq}}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{1},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}}\right)\leq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2}\|{f_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\|{u}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}}\right),

(100)

where the last step follows from Cauchy-Schwarz, and thus
\psi_{1}
is sub-Gaussian with parameter
\sigma_{1}=\|{f_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}
as desired.

Inductive step

Fix any
i\in[n]
with
i\geq 2
and assume that the functional sub-Gaussianity claim
Eq.

13
holds
for
\psi_{i-1}
with
\sigma_{i-1}
. We have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right)]

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right)|\psi_{i-1}]]

(101)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:temprv_defn}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i-1},u-f_{i}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right)|\psi_{i-1}]}}\right]

(102)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:temprv_constraints}}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i-1},u-f_{i}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right)\cdot\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}}\right)}}\right]

(103)

\textstyle=\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i-1},u-f_{i}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right)}}\right]

(104)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{u-f_{i}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}}\right),

(105)

where step (i) follows from
the induction hypothesis.
Simplifying the exponent in the display
Eq.

105
using Cauchy-Schwarz and the definition
Eq.

12
of
\sigma_{i}
, we have

\textstyle\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{u-f_{i}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}

\textstyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{u}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f_{i}}}}\right\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{2}}-\frac{2}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right)}}\right)

(106)

\textstyle=\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{u}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f_{i}}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{2\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{2}}-\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right)

(107)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{u}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},u}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f_{i}}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{2\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{2}}-\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right)_{+}

(108)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\|{u}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sigma_{i-1}^{2}+\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\Big(1+\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{2}}(\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}-2\mathfrak{a}_{i})\Big)_{+}}}\right)

(109)

\textstyle=\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{u}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}.

(110)

J.2
Property
(ii)
: Signed sum representation

Since
Algorithm

5
adds
\pm f_{i}
to
\psi_{i-1}
whenever

|\alpha_{i}|=|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i-1},f_{i}}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|\leq\mathfrak{a}_{i}
, by the union bound, it suffices to lower bound the probability of this event by
1-\delta_{i}
for each
i
.
The following lemma
establishes this bound using the functional sub-Gaussianity
Eq.

13
of each
\psi_{i-1}
.

Lemma 10

(Self-balancing Hilbert walk success probability)
.

The self-balancing Hilbert walk (
Algorithm

5
) with
threshold

\mathfrak{a}_{i}\geq\sigma_{i-1}\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{2\log(2/\delta_{i})}

for
\delta_{i}\in(0,1]

satisfies

\textstyle\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{i})\geq 1-\delta_{i}\quad\text{for}\quad\mathcal{E}_{i}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i-1},f_{i}}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right|\leq\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}\right\}.

(111)

Proof

Instantiate the notation of
Theorem

3
.
The sub-Gaussian Hoeffding inequality
(
Wainwright 2019
, Prop. 2.5)
, the functional sub-Gaussianity of
\psi_{i-1}

Eq.

13
, and the choice of
\mathfrak{a}_{i}
imply that

\textstyle\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{c})=\mathrm{Pr}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i-1},f_{i}}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right|>\mathfrak{a}_{i})

\textstyle\leq 2\exp(-\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{2}/(2\sigma_{i-1}^{2}\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}))\leq 2\exp(-\log(2/\delta_{i}))=\delta_{i}.

(112)

J.3
Property
(iii)
: Exact halving via symmetrization

Whenever the signed sum representation
Eq.

14
holds, we have

\textstyle\psi_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}f_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\eta_{i}g_{2i-1}-\eta_{i}g_{2i})=\sum_{i=1}^{2n}g_{i}-2\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}g_{i}

(113)

where the last step follows from the definition of
\mathcal{I}
.

J.4
Property
(iv)
: Pointwise sub-Gaussianity in RKHS

The reproducing property of the kernel
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
and the established functional sub-Gaussianity
Eq.

13
yield

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(\psi_{i}(x))]=\mathbb{E}[\exp(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{i},{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,\cdot)}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}})]\leq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}\|{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,\cdot)}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{2}}}\right)=\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,x)}{2}}}\right),\quad\forall x\in\mathcal{X}.

(114)

J.5
Property
(v)
: Sub-Gaussian constant bound

We establish the bound
Eq.

17

for all
i\in\{0,\dots,n\}
by induction on the iteration
i
.

Base case

The claim
Eq.

17
holds for the base case,
i=0
, since
\sigma_{0}=0
.

Inductive step

Fix any
i\in[n]
and assume that the claim
Eq.

17
holds
for
\sigma_{i-1}
.

If either
\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}=0
or
\sigma_{i-1}^{2}\geq\frac{\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{2}}{2\mathfrak{a}_{i}-\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}
,
then
\sigma_{i}^{2}=\sigma_{i-1}^{2}
by the definition
Eq.

12
of
\sigma_{i}
and the assumption that
\frac{\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{2}\leq\mathfrak{a}_{i}
, completing the inductive step.

If, alternatively,
\sigma_{i-1}^{2}<\frac{\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{2}}{2\mathfrak{a}_{i}-\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}
and
\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}>0
, then our assumptions
\frac{\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{1+q}\leq\mathfrak{a}_{i}\leq\frac{\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{1-q}

imply that
(\frac{\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}-1)^{2}\leq q^{2}
.
Hence, by the definition
Eq.

12
of
\sigma_{i}

and the inductive hypothesis,

\textstyle\sigma_{i}^{2}

\textstyle=\sigma_{i-1}^{2}+\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\Big(1+\frac{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{2}}(\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}-2\mathfrak{a}_{i})\Big)

(115)

\textstyle=\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\sigma_{i-1}^{2}(\frac{\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}-1)^{2}

(116)

\textstyle\leq(1-q^{2})\frac{\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{1-q^{2}}+q^{2}\frac{\max_{j\in[i-1]}\|{f_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{1-q^{2}}\leq\frac{\max_{j\in[i]}\|{f_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{1-q^{2}},

(117)

completing the inductive step.

J.6
Property
(vi)
: Adaptive thresholding

Define
c^{\star}_{1}=\max(c^{\star},1)
and
let

q=\frac{(c^{\star}_{1})^{2}-1}{(c^{\star}_{1})^{2}+1}\in[0,1)

so that

\textstyle\frac{1}{1-q}=\frac{1+(c^{\star}_{1})^{2}}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{1-q^{2}}=\frac{(c^{\star}_{1}+1/c^{\star}_{1})^{2}}{4}\leq\frac{(c^{\star}+1/c^{\star})^{2}}{4},

(118)

since
1=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{argmin}_{c\geq 0}c+1/c
.
By assumption,

\mathfrak{a}_{i}\geq\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\geq\frac{\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{1+q}

for all
i\in[n]
.

Now suppose that
\sigma_{i-1}^{2}<\frac{\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{2}}{2\mathfrak{a}_{i}-\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}

and

\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}>0
.
If
\mathfrak{a}_{i}\leq\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
, then
\mathfrak{a}_{i}\leq\frac{\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{1-q}
.
If, alternatively,
\mathfrak{a}_{i}\leq c_{i}\sigma_{i-1}\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}
, then

\textstyle\mathfrak{a}_{i}<\frac{1}{2}\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}(1+c_{i}^{2})\leq\frac{\|{f_{i}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}{1-q}.

(119)

The conclusion now follows from the sub-Gaussian constant bound
Eq.

17
.

J.7
Proof of
Lemma

9
: Alternate representation of
\psi_{i}

Algorithm

5
and our definition
\varepsilon_{i}\triangleq\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\alpha_{i}}}\right|\leq\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right](\eta_{i}+\alpha_{i}/\mathfrak{a}_{i})
give

\textstyle\psi_{i}=\psi_{i-1}-f_{i}\,\alpha_{i}/\mathfrak{a}_{i}+\mathbb{I}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\alpha_{i}}}\right|\leq\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right](f_{i}\,\alpha_{i}/\mathfrak{a}_{i}+\eta_{i}f_{i})=\psi_{i-1}-f_{i}\,\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle f_{i},\psi_{i-1}}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}+\varepsilon_{i}f_{i}.

(120)

Taking an inner product with
u\in\mathcal{H}
now yields the equality
Eq.

98
.
By construction,
\varepsilon_{i}\in[c_{min},c_{max}]\subseteq[-2,2]
for

\textstyle c_{min}=\max(-2,\min(0,-1+\alpha_{i}/\mathfrak{a}_{i}))\quad\text{and}\quad c_{max}=\min(2,\max(0,1+\alpha_{i}/\mathfrak{a}_{i}))

(121)

by construction.
Moreover,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}\mid\psi_{i-1},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\alpha_{i}}}\right|>\mathfrak{a}_{i}]

\textstyle=0

(122)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}\mid\psi_{i-1},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\alpha_{i}}}\right|\leq\mathfrak{a}_{i}]

\textstyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{\alpha_{i}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{i}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right)+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-1+\frac{\alpha_{i}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{\alpha_{i}}{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}}}\right)=0,

(123)

so that
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}\mid\psi_{i-1}]=0
as claimed.
The conditional sub-Gaussianity claim

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[e^{t\varepsilon_{i}}\mid\psi_{i-1}]\leq e^{t^{2}/2}\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in\mathbb{R},

(124)

now follows from Hoeffding’s lemma
(
Hoeffding 1963
, (4.16))
since
\varepsilon_{i}
is bounded with

c_{max}-c_{min}\leq 2

and mean-zero conditional on
\psi_{i-1}
.

Appendix K
Proof of
Theorem

4
:
L^{\infty}
guarantees for kernel halving

We start by showing that after
m
rounds of kernel halving the output size is
n_{\mathrm{out}}=\lfloor{\frac{n}{2^{m}}}\rfloor
(also see
Footnote

4
). We prove this by induction. The base case of
m=1
can be directly verified. Let
n_{j}\triangleq|\mathcal{S}^{(j)}|
denote the output size after
j
rounds of kernel halving and assume that the hypothesis is true for round
j
, i.e.,
n_{j}=\lfloor{\frac{n}{2^{j}}}\rfloor
. Then for round
j+1
, we have
n_{j+1}=\lfloor{n_{j}/2}\rfloor=\lfloor{\frac{\lfloor{n/2^{j}}\rfloor}{2}}\rfloor\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\lfloor{\frac{n}{2^{j+1}}}\rfloor
, where step (i) follows from the fact
(
Graham et al. 1994
, Eqn. (3.11))
that
\lfloor{\frac{\lfloor{\ell}\rfloor}{2}}\rfloor=\lfloor{\frac{\ell}{2}}\rfloor
for any integer
\ell
.
Our desired claim follows.

Next, define
n_{\mathrm{in}}=2^{m}n_{\mathrm{out}}=2^{m}\lfloor{\frac{n}{2^{m}}}\rfloor
.
In
Sections

K.1
and
K.2
we will show that the respective claims
Eqs.

22
and
23

hold whenever
n=n_{\mathrm{in}}
, that is, whenever
2^{m}
divides
n
evenly.
Now suppose that
n\neq n_{\mathrm{in}}
.
Since the output of
m
rounds of kernel halving depends only on the first
n_{\mathrm{in}}
points, the evenly divisible case of
Section

K.1
implies that,
for part
Item

(a)
,

\textstyle\|\mathbb{P}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{k}-{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathrm{KH}}^{(1)}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n_{\mathrm{in}},\!1,\!d,\!\delta^{\star},\!\delta^{\prime},\!R_{\mathcal{S}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}},\mathbf{k},n_{\mathrm{in}}})\ \text{for}\ n_{\mathrm{in}}=2\lfloor{\frac{n}{2}}\rfloor=2n_{\mathrm{out}},

(125)

with probability at least
1\!-\!\delta^{\prime}\!-\!\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{in}}/2}\delta_{i}
, and
Section

K.1
implies that, for part
Item

(b)
,

\textstyle\|\mathbb{P}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{k}\!-\!\mathbb{Q}^{(m)}_{\mathrm{KH}}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n_{\mathrm{in}},\!m,\!d,\!\delta^{\star},\!\delta^{\prime},\!R_{\mathcal{S}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}},\mathbf{k},n_{\mathrm{in}}})\ \text{for}\ n_{\mathrm{in}}\!=\!2^{m}\lfloor{\frac{n}{2^{m}}}\rfloor\!=\!2^{m}n_{\mathrm{out}},

(126)

with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}\!-\!\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2^{j-1}}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{in}}/2^{j}}\delta_{i}
. Next, to recover the bounds
Eqs.

22
and
23
, we use the following deterministic inequalities:

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-\mathbb{P}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\leq\frac{2(n-n_{\mathrm{in}})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{n}\leq\frac{2(2^{m}-1)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{n}\leq\frac{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}},

(127)

\textstyle R_{\mathcal{S}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}},\mathbf{k},n_{\mathrm{in}}}

\textstyle\leq R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n},\quad\text{and}\quad

(128)

\textstyle\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n_{\mathrm{in}},\!m,\!d,\!\delta,\!\delta^{\prime},\!R_{\mathcal{S}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}},\mathbf{k},n_{\mathrm{in}}})\!+\!2

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n_{\mathrm{in}},\!m,d,\!\delta,\!\delta^{\prime},\!R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n})\!+\!2

(129)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,\!m,\!d,\!\delta,\!\delta^{\prime},\!R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}),

(130)

where
Eq.

128
follows directly from the definition
Eq.

7
, and step (i) follows from
Eq.

128
and the fact that
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,\!m,\!d,\!\delta,\!\delta^{\prime},\!R)

Eq.

8
is non-decreasing in
R
, and step (ii) follows since
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,\!m,\!d,\!\delta,\!\delta^{\prime},\!R)
is non-decreasing in
n
and
n_{\mathrm{in}}\neq n
.
For part
Item

(a)
we conclude, by
Eq.

125
,

\textstyle\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}\!-\!\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{KH}}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-\mathbb{P}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}+\|\mathbb{P}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{k}\!-\!\mathbb{Q}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{KH}}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}

(131)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:perr_nin}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+\frac{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n_{\mathrm{in}},1,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}},\mathbf{k},n_{\mathrm{in}}})

(132)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:err_err_nin}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,1,d,\delta,\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}).

(133)

For part
Item

(b)
, we conclude, by
Eq.

126
,

\textstyle\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}\!-\!\mathbb{Q}^{(m)}_{\mathrm{KH}}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-\mathbb{P}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}+\|\mathbb{P}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}}\mathbf{k}\!-\!\mathbb{Q}^{(m)}_{\mathrm{KH}}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}

(134)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:perr_nin}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}+\frac{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n_{\mathrm{in}},1,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n_{\mathrm{in}}},\mathbf{k},n_{\mathrm{in}}})

(135)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:err_err_nin}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,m,d,\delta,\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}).

(136)

K.1
Proof of part
Item

(a)
: Kernel halving yields a
2
-thinned
L^{\infty}
coreset

As noted earlier, we prove this part assuming
n
is even.
Consider a self-balancing Hilbert walk (
Algorithm

5
) with inputs
(f_{i})_{i=1}^{n/2}
and
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n/2}
, where
f_{i}=\mathbf{k}(x_{2i-1},\cdot)-\mathbf{k}(x_{2i},\cdot)
and
\mathfrak{a}_{i}=\max(\|{f_{i}}\|_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}\sigma_{i-1}\sqrt{2\log(2/\delta_{i})},\|{f_{i}}\|_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}^{2})
, and
\sigma_{i}
was defined in
Eq.

12
.
Property
(iii)
of
Theorem

3
implies that for a self-balancing walk with the choices summarized above, the event

\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}=\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\alpha_{i}}}\right|\leq\mathfrak{a}_{i}\ \text{for all}\ i\in[{n/2}]\}
satisfies

\textstyle\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}})\geq 1-\sum_{i=1}^{{n/2}}\delta_{i}.

(137)

Consider kernel halving coupled with the above instantiation of self-balancing Hilbert walk.
Due to the equivalence with kernel halving on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}
, we conclude that the output
\psi_{n/2}
of self-balancing Hilbert walk matches with that of the kernel halving, and satisfies

\textstyle\frac{1}{n}\psi_{n/2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}(x_{i},\cdot)-\frac{1}{{n/2}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{S}^{(1)}}\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)=\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathrm{KH}}^{(1)}\mathbf{k},

(138)

on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}
.
Furthermore, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}
, we can also write that the kernel halving coreset satisfies
\mathcal{S}^{(1)}=(x_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
for
\mathcal{I}=\{2i-\frac{\eta_{i}-1}{2}:i\in[n/2]\}
and
\eta_{i}
defined in
Algorithm

5
.
Finally, applying property
(vi)
of
Theorem

3
for
\sigma_{n/2}
with
c_{i}=\sqrt{2\log(2/\delta_{i})}
, we obtain that

\textstyle\sigma^{2}_{n/2}\leq\frac{\max_{i\leq n/2}\|{f_{i}}\|_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}^{2}}{4}\cdot 2\log(\frac{2}{\delta^{\star}})(1+\frac{1}{2\log(2/\delta^{\star})})^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}4\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\log(\frac{2}{\delta^{\star}})

(139)

where in step (i), we use the fact that
f_{i}=\mathbf{k}(x_{2i-1},\cdot)-\mathbf{k}(x_{2i},\cdot)
satisfies

\textstyle\|{f_{i}}\|_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}^{2}=\mathbf{k}(x_{2i-1},x_{2i-1})+\mathbf{k}(x_{2i},x_{2i})-2\mathbf{k}(x_{2i-1},x_{2i})\leq 4\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}.

(140)

Next, we split the proof in two parts:
Case (I)
When
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}<R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}
, and
Case (II)
when
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}=R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}
, where
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}
was defined in
Eq.

7
. We prove the results for these two cases in
Sections

K.1.1
and
K.1.2
respectively.
In the sequel, we make use of the following tail quantity of the kernel:

\textstyle\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R^{\prime})\triangleq\sup\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x,y)}}\right|:\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq R^{\prime}}}\right\}.

(141)

K.1.1
Proof for case (I): When
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}<R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}

By definition
Eq.

7
, for this case,

\textstyle R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}=n^{1+\frac{1}{d}}R_{\mathbf{k},n}+n^{\frac{1}{d}}\frac{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}{L_{\mathbf{k}}}.

(142)

On the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}
, the following lemma provides a high probability bound on
\|\psi_{n/2}\|_{\infty}
in terms of the kernel parameters, the sub-Gaussianity parameter
\sigma_{n/2}
, and the size of the
cover

(
Wainwright 2019
, Def. 5.1)
of a neighborhood of the input points
(x_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
.

Lemma 11

(A direct covering bound on
\|\psi_{n/2}\|_{\infty}
)
.

Fix
R\geq r>0
and
\delta^{\prime}>0
, and suppose
\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)
is a set of minimum cardinality satisfying

\textstyle\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{B}(x_{i},R)\subseteq\bigcup_{z\in\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)}\mathcal{B}(z,r).

(143)

If
\mathbf{k}
satisfies
1
and
2
, then, conditional on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}

Eq.

138
, the event

\textstyle\mathcal{E}_{\infty}\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|\psi_{n/2}\|_{\infty}\leq\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R),\ \ nL_{\mathbf{k}}r+\sigma_{n/2}\sqrt{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\log(2|\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)|/\delta^{\prime})}}}\right)}}\right\},

(144)

occurs with probability at least
1\!-\!\delta^{\prime}
, i.e.,
\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\infty}|\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}})\geq 1\!-\!\delta^{\prime}
, where
\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}
was defined in
Eq.

141
.

Lemma

11
succeeds in controlling
\psi_{n/2}(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
since either
x
lies far from every input point
x_{i}
so that each
\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)
in the expansion
Eq.

138
is small or
x
lies near some
x_{i}
, in which case
\psi_{n/2}(x)
is well approximated by
\psi_{n/2}(z)
for
z\in\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)
.
The proof inspired by the covering argument of
Phillips and Tai 2020
and using the pointwise sub-Gaussianity property of
Theorem

3
over the finite cover
\mathcal{C}^{n}
can be found in
Section

K.3
.

Now we put together the pieces to prove
Theorem

4
.

First,
(
Wainwright 2019
, Lem. 5.7)
implies that

|\mathcal{C}^{1}(r,R)|\leq(1+2R/r)^{d}
(i.e., any ball of radius
R
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
can be covered by
(1+2R/r)^{d}
balls of radius
r
). Thus for an arbitrary
R
, we can conclude that

\textstyle|\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)|\leq n(1+2R/r)^{d}=(n^{1/d}+2n^{1/d}R/r)^{d}.

(145)

Second we fix
R
and
r
such that
n\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}
and
nL_{\mathbf{k}}r=\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}
, so that
\frac{R}{r}\leftarrow nR_{\mathbf{k},n}\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}
(c.f.
Eqs.

141
and
7
). Substituting these choices of radii in the bound
Eq.

144
of
Lemma

11
, we find that conditional to
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\infty}
, we have

\textstyle\|\psi_{n/2}\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\leq\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R),\ \ nL_{\mathbf{k}}r+\sigma_{n/2}\sqrt{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\log(2|\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)|/\delta^{\prime})}}}\right)

(146)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:sigma_bound}}}{{\leq}}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}+2\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\log(\frac{2}{\delta^{\star}})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log(\frac{2}{\delta^{\prime}})+d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n^{\frac{1}{d}}+\frac{2L_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}\cdot n^{1+\frac{1}{d}}R_{\mathbf{k},n}}}\right)}}\right]}

(147)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:rmin_new_case_a}}}{{\leq}}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}+2\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\log(\frac{2}{\delta^{\star}})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log(\frac{2}{\delta^{\prime}})+d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2L_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}(R_{\mathbf{k},n}+R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n})}}\right)}}\right]}

(148)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:err_simple_defn}}}{{\leq}}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\cdot 2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,1,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}).

(149)

Putting
Eqs.

138
and
149
together, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}\big(\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathrm{KH}}^{(1)}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}>\frac{1}{n/2}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,1,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Pr}((\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\infty})^{c})

(150)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{c}\cup\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{c})

(151)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{c})

(152)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{c}|\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}})

(153)

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{\prime}+{\textstyle\sum}_{i=1}^{{{n/2}}}\delta_{i},

(154)

where the last step follows from
Eqs.

137
and
11
. The claim now follows.

K.1.2
Proof for case (II): When
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}=R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}

In this case, we split the proof for bounding
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\psi_{n/2}}}}\right\|_{\infty}
into two lemmas.
First, we relate the
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\psi_{n/2}}}}\right\|_{\infty}
in terms of the tail behavior of
\mathbf{k}
and the supremum of differences for
\psi_{n/2}
between any pair points on a Euclidean ball (see
Section

K.4
for the proof):

Lemma 12

(A basic bound on
\|\psi_{n/2}\|_{\infty}
)
.

If
\mathbf{k}
satisfies
1
,
then, conditional on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}

Eq.

138
,
for any fixed
R=R^{\prime}+R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}
with
R^{\prime}>0
and any fixed
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}\!=\!\bigg\{\|\psi_{n/2}\|_{\infty}\!\leq\!\max\!\big(n\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R^{\prime}),\sigma_{n/2}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2\log({\textstyle\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}}})}\!+\!\!\!\!\sup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)}\!\!\!\!\!|{\psi_{n/2}(x)\!-\!\psi_{n/2}(x^{\prime})}|\big)\bigg\}

(155)

occurs with probability at least
1\!-\!\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}
, i.e.,
\mathrm{Pr}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}|\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}})\!\geq\!1\!-\!\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}
, where
\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}
was defined in
Eq.

141
.

Next, to control the supremum term on the RHS of the display
Eq.

155
, we establish a high probability bound in the next lemma. Its proof in
Section

K.5
proceeds by showing that
\psi_{n/2}
is an Orlicz process with a suitable metric and then applying standard concentration arguments for such processes.

Lemma 13

(A high probability bound on supremum of
\psi_{n/2}
differences)
.

If
\mathbf{k}
satisfies
1
and
2
, then, for any fixed
R>0,\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
, the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}\triangleq\bigg\{\sup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)}|{\psi_{n/2}(x)\!-\!\psi_{n/2}(x^{\prime})}|\!\leq\!8D_{R}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\log({\textstyle\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}}})}+6\sqrt{d\log\big(2+{\textstyle\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}R}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}\big)}}}\right)\bigg\}

(156)

occurs with probability at least

1-\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}
,
where

D_{R}\triangleq\sqrt{\frac{32}{3}}\sigma_{n/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\min\big(1,\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{k}}R}\big)
.

We now turn to the rest of the proof for
Theorem

4
. We apply both
Lemmas

12
and
13
with
R=R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}+R_{\mathbf{k},n}

Eqs.

7
and
7
.
For this
R
, we have
R^{\prime}=R_{\mathbf{k},n}
in
Lemma

12
and hence
n\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R^{\prime})\leq\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}
.
Now, condition on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}
. Then, we have

\textstyle n\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-{\mathbb{Q}}_{\textrm{KH}}^{(1)}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}

(157)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{outvec_as_kernel_diff}}}{{=}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\psi_{n/2}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

(158)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:infnorm_basic_bound}}}{{\leq}}\max(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty},\ \sigma_{n/2}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2\log(\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}})}+\sup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)}|{\psi_{n/2}(x)\!-\!\psi_{n/2}(x^{\prime})}|)

(159)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:orlicz_hpbound_lemma}}}{{\leq}}\max\Big(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty},\ \sigma_{n/2}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2\log(\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}})}+

(160)

\textstyle\hskip 48.0pt32\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\sigma_{n/2}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\log(\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}})}+6\sqrt{d\log(2+\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}+R_{\mathbf{k},n})}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}})}}}\right)\Big)

(161)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\max(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty},\ 32\sigma_{n/2}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\log(\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}})}+5\sqrt{d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2+\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}+R_{\mathbf{k},n})}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}}\right)}}}\right))

(162)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:sigma_bound}}}{{\leq}}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\cdot 64\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{\delta^{\star}}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sqrt{\log(\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}})}+5\sqrt{d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2+\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}+R_{\mathbf{k},n})}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}}\right)}}}\right]

(163)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}2\,\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,1,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}),

(164)

where step (i) follows from the fact that
D_{R}\leq\sqrt{\frac{32}{3}}\sigma_{n/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}
, and in step (ii) we have
used the working assumption for this case, i.e.,
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}=R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}
.
As a result,

\textstyle\mathrm{Pr}(\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}\!-\!{\mathbb{Q}}_{\textrm{KH}}^{(1)}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\!>\!\frac{2}{n}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,\!1,\!d,\!\delta^{\star},\!\delta^{\prime},\!R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n})\!

\textstyle\leq\mathrm{Pr}((\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}})^{c})

(165)

\textstyle=\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{c}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}^{c}\cup\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}^{c})

(166)

\textstyle=\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{c}\cup\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}((\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{c}\cup\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}^{c})^{c}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}^{c})

(167)

\textstyle=\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{c}\cup\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}^{c})

(168)

\textstyle\leq\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{c}\cup\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}^{c})

(169)

\textstyle\leq\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}^{c}|\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}})

(170)

\textstyle\leq{\textstyle\sum}_{i=1}^{n/2}\delta_{i}+\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}+\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2},

(171)

where the last step follows from
Eqs.

137
,
12
, and
13
. The desired claim follows.

K.2
Proof of part
Item

(b)
: Repeated kernel halving yields a
2^{m}
-thinned
L^{\infty}
coreset

As noted earlier, we prove this part assuming
n/2^{m}\in\mathbb{N}
. The proof in this section follows by applying the arguments from the previous section, separately for each round and then invoking the sub-Gaussianity of a weighted sum of the output functions from each round.

Note that coreset
\mathcal{S}^{(j-1)}
is independent of the randomness for the
j
-th round of kernel havling and thus can be treated as fixed for that round. When running kernel halving with the input
\mathcal{S}^{(j-1)}
, let
f_{i,j},\mathfrak{a}_{i,j},\psi_{i,j},\alpha_{i,j},\eta_{i,j}
denote the analog of the quantities
f_{i},\mathfrak{a}_{i},\psi_{i},\alpha_{i},\eta_{i}
defined in
Algorithm

3
. Like in part
Item

(a)
, we would couple the
j
-th round of kernel halving with an instantiation of self-balancing Hilbert walk with inputs
(f_{i,j},\mathfrak{a}_{i,j})_{i=1}^{n/2^{j}}
and final output
\psi_{n/2^{j},j}
, where
\mathfrak{a}_{i,j}=\max(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f_{i,j}}}}\right\|_{\mathbf{k}}\sigma_{i-1,j}\sqrt{2\log(\frac{4m}{2^{j}\delta_{i}})},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f_{i,j}}}}\right\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2})
and we define
\sigma_{i,j}
in a recursive manner as in
Eq.

12
with
f_{i,j},\mathfrak{a}_{i,j},\sigma_{i,j}
taking the role of
f_{i},\mathfrak{a}_{i},\sigma_{i}
respectively.

With this set-up, first we apply property
(i)
of
Theorem

3
which implies that given
\mathcal{S}^{(j-1)}
, the function
\psi_{n/2^{j},j}
is
\sigma_{n/2^{j},j}
sub-Gaussian, where

\textstyle\sigma_{n/2^{j},j}^{2}\leq 4\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\log(\frac{4m}{2^{j}\delta^{\star}_{j}})\quad\text{with}\quad\delta^{\star}_{j}=\min(\delta_{i})_{i=1}^{n/2^{j}},

(172)

using an argument similar to
Eq.

139
with property
(vi)
of
Theorem

3
.
Next, we note that for
j
-th round, the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\alpha_{i,j}}}\right|\leq\mathfrak{a}_{i,j}
for all
i\in[n/2^{j}]
, satisfies

\textstyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)})\geq 1-\sum_{i=1}^{{n/2^{j}}}\delta_{i}\frac{2^{j-1}}{m};

(173)

and this event serves as the analog the equivalence event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}
from part
Item

(a)
for the
j
-th round of kernel halving. Thus on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}
, we can write that the kernel halving coreset
\mathcal{S}^{(j)}=(x_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{j}}
for
\mathcal{I}_{j}=\{2i-\frac{\eta_{i,j}-1}{2}:i\in[n/2^{j}]\}
and
\eta_{i,j}
as defined while running
Algorithm

5
, so that

\textstyle\frac{2^{j-1}}{n}\psi_{n/2^{j},j}=\frac{1}{n/2^{j-1}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{S}^{(j-1)}}\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)-\frac{1}{{n/2^{j}}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{S}^{(j)}}\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot).

(174)

Now conditional on the event
\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}
, we conclude that the output of all
m
kernel halving rounds are equivalent to the output of
m
different self-balancing Hilbert walks (each with exact two-thinning in each round). Putting the pieces together, we conclude that on the event
\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{KH}}^{(m)}\mathbf{k}

\textstyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)-\frac{1}{{n/2^{m}}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{S}^{(m)}}\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)

(175)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{n/2^{j-1}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{S}^{(j-1)}}\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)-\frac{1}{{n/2^{j}}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{S}^{(j)}}\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)

(176)

\textstyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2^{j-1}}{n}\psi_{n/2^{j},j}.

(177)

where we abuse notation
\mathcal{S}^{(0)}\triangleq\mathcal{S}_{n}
in step (i) for simplicity of expressions.

Next, we use the following basic fact:

Lemma 14

(Sub-Gaussian additivity)
.

For a sequence of random variables
(Z_{j})_{j=1}^{m}
such that
Z_{j}
is a
\sigma_{j}
sub-Gaussian variable conditional on
(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{j-1})
, the random variable
\mathcal{Z}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\theta_{j}Z_{j}
is
(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\theta_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2})^{1/2}
sub-Gaussian.

Proof

We will prove the result for
\mathcal{Z}_{s}=\sum_{j=1}^{s}\theta_{j}Z_{j}
by induction on
s\leq m
.
The result holds for the base case of
s=0
as
\mathcal{Z}_{s}=0
is
0
sub-Gaussian.
For the inductive case, suppose the result holds for
s
.
Then we may apply the tower property, our conditional sub-Gaussianity assumption, and our inductive hypothesis in turn to conclude

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[e^{\sum_{j=1}^{s+1}\theta_{j}Z_{j}}]=\mathbb{E}[e^{\sum_{j=1}^{s+1}\theta_{j}Z_{j}}\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta_{s+1}Z_{s+1}}\mid Z_{1:s}]]\leq\mathbb{E}[e^{\sum_{j=1}^{s}\theta_{j}Z_{j}}]e^{\frac{\theta_{s+1}^{2}\sigma_{s+1}^{2}}{2}}=e^{\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{s+1}\theta_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}}{2}}.

(178)

Hence,
\mathcal{Z}_{s+1}
is
\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{s+1}\theta_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}}
sub-Gaussian, and the proof is complete.

Applying
Lemma

14
to the output sequence
(\psi_{n/2^{j},j})_{j=1}^{m}
for the
m
rounds of self-balancing Hilbert walks, we conclude that,
the random variable

\textstyle\mathcal{W}_{m}\triangleq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2^{j-1}}{n}\psi_{n/2^{j},j}

(179)

is sub-Gaussian with parameter

\textstyle\sigma_{\mathcal{W}_{m}}\!\triangleq\!\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\frac{2^{m}}{n}\sqrt{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\log(\frac{6m}{2^{m}\delta^{\star}})}\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\geq}}\!\sqrt{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{4^{j}}{n^{2}}\log(\frac{4m}{2^{j}\delta^{\star}})}\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\geq}}\!\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{m}(\frac{2^{j-1}}{n})^{2}\sigma_{n/2^{j},j}^{2}},

(180)

conditional to the input
\mathcal{S}_{n}
,
where step (ii) follows since

\textstyle\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{m}4^{j}\log(\frac{4m}{2^{j}\delta^{\star}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\geq}}4\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{m}4^{j-1}\log(\frac{4m}{2^{j}\delta^{\star}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:sigmaj_bound}}}{{\geq}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}4^{j-1}\sigma_{n/2^{j},j}^{2},

(181)

and step (iii) follows from the fact that
\delta^{\star}=\min(\delta^{\star}_{j})_{j=1}^{m}

Eq.

172
. Now to prove step (i), we note that

\textstyle\mathfrak{S}_{1}\triangleq\sum_{j=1}^{m}4^{j}=\frac{4}{3}(4^{m}-1)\leq\frac{4}{3}4^{m},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{S}_{2}\triangleq\sum_{j=1}^{m}j4^{j}=\frac{4m}{3}\cdot 4^{m}-\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}{3},

(182)

which in turn implies that

\textstyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}4^{j}\log(\frac{4m}{2^{j}\delta^{\star}})\!=\!\mathfrak{S}_{1}\log(\frac{4m}{\delta^{\star}})\!-\!\log 2\cdot\mathfrak{S}_{2}

\textstyle=\mathfrak{S}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log(\frac{4m}{\delta^{\star}})\!+\!\frac{\log 2}{3}}}\right)\!-\!\frac{4m\log 2}{3}\cdot 4^{m}

(183)

\textstyle\leq\mathfrak{S}_{1}\cdot\log(\frac{6m}{\delta^{\star}})\!-\!\frac{4m\log 2}{3}\cdot 4^{m}

(184)

\textstyle\leq\frac{4}{3}4^{m}\cdot\log(\frac{6m}{\delta^{\star}})\!-\!\frac{4}{3}4^{m}\cdot\log 2^{m}\!=\!\frac{4}{3}4^{m}\cdot\log(\frac{6m}{2^{m}\delta^{\star}}),

(185)

thereby establishing step (i).

Next, analogous to the proof of part
Item

(a)
, we split the proof in two parts:
Case (I)
When
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}<R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}
, in which case, we proceed with a direct covering argument to bound
\|\mathcal{W}_{m}\|_{\infty}
using a lemma analogous to
Lemma

11
; and
Case (II)
when
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}=R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}
, in which case, we proceed with a metric-entropy based argument to bound
\|\mathcal{W}_{m}\|_{\infty}
using two lemmas that are analogous
Lemmas

12
and
13
.

K.2.1
Proof for case (I): When
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}<R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}

Recall that for this case,
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}
is given by
Eq.

142
. The next lemma, a straightforward extension of
Lemma

11
, provides a high probability control on
\|\mathcal{W}_{m}\|_{\infty}
. Its proof is omitted for brevity.

Lemma 15

(A direct covering bound on
\|\mathcal{W}_{m}\|_{\infty}
)
.

Fix
R\geq r>0
and
\delta^{\prime}>0
, and recall the definition
Eq.

143
of
\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)
.
If
\mathbf{k}
satisfies
1
and
2
, then, conditional on the event
\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}

Eq.

174
, the event

\textstyle\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{(m)}\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|\mathcal{W}_{m}\|_{\infty}\leq\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2^{m}\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R),\ \ 2^{m}L_{\mathbf{k}}r+\sigma_{\mathcal{W}_{m}}\sqrt{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\log(2|\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)|/\delta^{\prime})}}}\right)}}\right\},

(186)

occurs with probability at least
1\!-\!\delta^{\prime}
, i.e.,
\mathrm{Pr}(\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{(m)}|\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)})\geq 1\!-\!\delta^{\prime}
.

Next, we repeat arguments similar to those used earlier around the display
Eqs.

147
and
149
.
Fix
R
and
r
such that
\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}/n
and
L_{\mathbf{k}}r=\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}/n
, so that
\frac{R}{r}\leftarrow nR_{\mathbf{k},n}\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}
(c.f.
Eqs.

141
and
7
). Substituting these choices of radii in the bound
Eq.

186
of
Lemma

15
, we find that conditional to
\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{(m)}
, we have

\textstyle\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{KH}}^{(m)}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:diff_as_sum_of_psi}}}{{=}}\|\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2^{j-1}}{n}\psi_{n/2^{j},j}\|_{\infty}

(187)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:sumpsi_def}}}{{=}}\|\mathcal{W}_{m}\|_{\infty}

(188)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:multi_round_einf_direct_cover}}}{{\leq}}\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2^{m}\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R),\ \ 2^{m}L_{\mathbf{k}}r+\sigma_{\mathcal{W}_{m}}\sqrt{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\log(2|\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)|/\delta^{\prime})}}}\right)

(189)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\frac{2^{m}}{n}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}(1+2\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\sqrt{\log(\frac{6m}{2^{m}\delta^{\star}})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log(\frac{2}{\delta^{\prime}})+d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n^{\frac{1}{d}}+\frac{2L_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}\cdot n^{1+\frac{1}{d}}R_{\mathbf{k},n}}}\right)}}\right]}

(190)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:rmin_new_case_a}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2^{m}}{n}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}(1+2\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\sqrt{\log(\frac{6m}{2^{m}\delta^{\star}})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log(\frac{2}{\delta^{\prime}})+d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2L_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}(R_{\mathbf{k},n}+R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n})}}\right)}}\right]})

(191)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:err_simple_defn}}}{{\leq}}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\cdot\frac{2^{m}}{n}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,m,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}),

(192)

where in step (i), we have used the bounds
Eqs.

180
and
145
.
Putting the pieces together, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}\big(\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathrm{KH}}^{(m)}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}>\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\cdot\frac{2^{m}}{n}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,m,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n})\big)

(193)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Pr}((\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{(m)})^{c})

(194)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Pr}(\cup_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)})^{c}\cup(\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{(m)})^{c})

(195)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Pr}(\cup_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)})^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}(\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{(m)})^{c})

(196)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathrm{Pr}((\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)})^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}((\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{(m)})^{c}|\cap_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}))

(197)

\displaystyle\leq{\textstyle\sum}_{j=1}^{m}{\textstyle\sum}_{i=1}^{{n/2^{j}}}\delta_{i}\cdot{\textstyle\frac{2^{j-1}}{m}}+\delta^{\prime},

(198)

where the last step follows from
Eqs.

173
and
15
.
The claim follows.

K.2.2
Proof for case (II): When
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathbf{k},n}=R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}

In this case, we makes use of two lemmas. Their proofs (omitted for brevity) can be derived essentially by replacing
\psi_{n/2}
and
\sigma_{n/2}
with
\mathcal{W}_{m}

Eq.

179
and
\sigma_{\mathcal{W}_{m}}

Eq.

180
respectively, and repeating the proof arguments from
Lemmas

12
and
13
.

Lemma 16

(A basic bound on
\|\mathcal{W}_{m}\|_{\infty}
)
.

If
\mathbf{k}
satisfies
1
, then, conditional on the event
\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}

Eq.

174
,
for any fixed
R=R^{\prime}+R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}
with
R^{\prime}>0
and any fixed
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}^{(m)}\!=\!\bigg\{\|\mathcal{W}_{m}\|_{\infty}\!\leq\!\max\bigg(2^{m}\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R^{\prime}),\ \sigma_{\mathcal{W}_{m}}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2\log(\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}})}\!+\!\sup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)}|{\mathcal{W}_{m}(x)\!-\!\mathcal{W}_{m}(x^{\prime})}|\bigg)\bigg\},

(199)

occurs with probability at least
1\!-\!\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}
, i.e.,
\mathrm{Pr}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}^{(m)}|\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)})\geq 1\!-\!\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}
.

Lemma 17

(A high probability bound on supremum of
\mathcal{W}_{m}
differences)
.

If
\mathbf{k}
satisfies
1
and
2
, then, for any fixed
R>0,\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
, given
\mathcal{S}_{n}
, the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}^{(m)}\triangleq\bigg\{\sup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)}|{\mathcal{W}_{m}(x)\!-\!\mathcal{W}_{m}(x^{\prime})}|\!\leq\!8D_{R}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\log({\textstyle\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}}})}+6\sqrt{d\log\big(2+{\textstyle\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}R}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}\big)}}}\right)\bigg\}

(200)

occurs with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}/2
, where
D_{R}\triangleq\sqrt{\frac{32}{3}}\sigma_{\mathcal{W}_{m}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\min\big(1,\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{k}}R}\big)
.

Mimicking the arguments like those in display
Eqs.

162
and
164
, with
R=R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}+R_{\mathbf{k},n}
, and
R^{\prime}=R_{\mathbf{k},n}
, we find that conditional on the event
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}^{(m)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}^{(m)}\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}
,

\textstyle\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{KH}}^{(m)}\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:diff_as_sum_of_psi}}}{{=}}\|\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2^{j-1}}{n}\psi_{n/2^{j},j}\|_{\infty}

(201)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:sumpsi_def}}}{{=}}\|\mathcal{W}_{m}\|_{\infty}

(202)

\textstyle\leq\max(\frac{2^{m}}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty},\ 32\sigma_{\mathcal{W}_{m}}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\log(\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}})}+5\sqrt{d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2+\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}+R_{\mathbf{k},n})}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}}\right)}}}\right))

(203)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:sgparam_bigpsi}}}{{\leq}}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\cdot\frac{2^{m}}{n}\cdot 37\sqrt{\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{6m}{2^{m}\delta^{\star}}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sqrt{\log(\frac{4}{\delta^{\prime}})}+5\sqrt{d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2+\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}+R_{\mathbf{k},n})}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}}\right)}}}\right]

(204)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:err_simple_defn}}}{{=}}\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}\cdot\frac{2^{m}}{n}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{k}}(n,m,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}),

(205)

which does not happed with probability at most

\textstyle\mathbb{P}((\cap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(m)}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}^{(m)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}^{(m)})^{c})

\textstyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathrm{Pr}((\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)})^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}((\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}^{(m)})^{c})+\mathrm{Pr}((\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}^{(m)})^{c}|\cap_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}^{(j)}))

(206)

\textstyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor{n/2^{j}}\rfloor}\delta_{i}\frac{2^{j-1}}{m}+\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}+\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2},

(207)

where the last step follows from
Eqs.

173
,
16
, and
17
as claimed. The proof is now complete.

K.3
Proof of
Lemma

11
: A direct covering bound on
\|\psi_{n/2}\|_{\infty}

We claim that conditional on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}

Eq.

138
,
we deterministically have

\textstyle\|\psi_{n/2}\|_{\infty}\leq\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{n\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R),\ \ nL_{\mathbf{k}}r+\max_{z\in\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)}|\psi_{n/2}(z)|}}\right\},

(208)

and the event

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\max_{z\in\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(z)}}\right|\leq\sigma_{n/2}\sqrt{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\log(2|\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)|/\delta^{\prime})}}}\right\}

(209)

occurs with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}
.
Putting these two claims together yields the lemma.
We now prove these two claims separately.

K.3.1
Proof of
Eq.

208

Note that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}

Eq.

138
we have

\textstyle\psi_{n/2}=n(\mathbb{P}_{n}\mathbf{k}-\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}_{n/2}\mathbf{k})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}\mathbf{k}(x_{i},\cdot)

(210)

for some
\eta_{i}\in\{-1,1\}
.
Now fix any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and introduce the shorthand
\mathcal{C}^{n}=\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)
.
The result follows by considering two cases.

(Case 1)
\boldsymbol{x\not\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{B}(x_{i},R)}

In this case, we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-x_{i}}}}\right\|_{2}\geq R
for all
i\in[n]
and therefore, representation
Eq.

210
yields that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(x)}}\right|=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)}}\right|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta_{i}}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)}}\right|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)}}\right|\leq n\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R),

(211)

by Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality and the definition
Eq.

141
of
\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}
.

(Case 2)
\boldsymbol{x\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{B}(x_{i},R)}

By the definition
Eq.

143
of our cover
\mathcal{C}^{n}
, there exists
z\in\mathcal{C}^{n}
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-z}}}\right\|_{2}\leq r
.
Therefore, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}
, using representation
Eq.

210
, we find that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(x)}}\right|\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)}}\right|

\textstyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)-\mathbf{k}(x_{i},z)+\mathbf{k}(x_{i},z)}}\right)}}\right|

(212)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)-\mathbf{k}(x_{i},z)}}\right)}}\right|+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}\mathbf{k}(x_{i},z)}}\right|

(213)

\textstyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)-\mathbf{k}(x_{i},z)}}\right|+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(z)}}\right|

(214)

\textstyle\leq nL_{\mathbf{k}}(r)+\sup_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{n}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(z^{\prime})}}\right|

(215)

by Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality.

K.3.2
Proof of
Eq.

209

Introduce the shorthand
\mathcal{C}^{n}=\mathcal{C}^{n}(r,R)
. Then applying the union bound, the pointwise sub-Gaussian property
(iv)
of
Theorem

3
, and the sub-Gaussian Hoeffding inequality
(
Wainwright 2019
, Prop. 2.5)
, we find that

\textstyle\mathrm{Pr}(\max_{z\in\mathcal{C}^{n}}|\psi_{n/2}(z)|>t\mid)

\textstyle\leq\sum_{z\in\mathcal{C}^{n}}2\exp(-t^{2}/(2\sigma_{n/2}^{2}\mathbf{k}(z,z)))

(216)

\textstyle\leq 2|\mathcal{C}^{n}|\exp(-t^{2}/(2\sigma_{n/2}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}))=\delta^{\prime}

(217)

\textstyle\quad\text{for}\quad t

\textstyle\triangleq\sigma_{n/2}\sqrt{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\log(2|\mathcal{C}^{n}|/\delta^{\prime})},

(218)

as claimed.

K.4
Proof of
Lemma

12
: A basic bound on
\|\psi_{n/2}\|_{\infty}

For any
R>0
, we deterministically have

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\psi_{n/2}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\max(\sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(x)}}\right|,\sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}^{c}(0,R)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(x)}}\right|)

(219)

Since
R=R^{\prime}+R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}
, for any
x\in\mathcal{B}^{c}(0,R)
, we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-x_{i}}}}\right\|_{2}\geq R^{\prime}
for all
i\in[n]
. Thus conditional on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{half}}
, applying property
(ii)
from
Theorem

3
, we find that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(x)}}\right|=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)}}\right|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\eta_{i}}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)}}\right|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbf{k}(x_{i},x)}}\right|\leq n\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}(R^{\prime}),

(220)

by Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality and the definition
Eq.

141
of
\overline{\tau}_{\mathbf{k}}
.

For the first term on the RHS of
Eq.

219
, we have

\textstyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(x)}}\right|

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(0)}}\right|+\sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(x)-\psi_{n/2}(0)}}\right|

(221)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(0)}}\right|+\sup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(x)-\psi_{n/2}(x^{\prime})}}\right|.

(222)

Now, the sub-Gaussianity of
\psi_{n/2}
(property
(iv)
of
Theorem

3
) with
x=0
, and the sub-Gaussian Hoeffding inequality
(
Wainwright 2019
, Prop. 2.5)
imply that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(0)}}\right|\leq\sigma_{n/2}\sqrt{2\mathbf{k}(0,0)\log(4/\delta)}\leq\sigma_{n/2}\sqrt{2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\log(4/\delta^{\prime})},

(223)

with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}/2
. Putting the pieces together completes the proof.

K.5
Proof of
Lemma

13
: A high probability bound on supremum of
\psi_{n/2}
differences

The proof proceeds by using concentration arguments for Orlicz processes
(
Wainwright 2019
, Def. 5.5)
. Given a set
\mathbb{T}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
, a random process
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{Z_{x},x\in\mathbb{T}}}\right\}
is called an Orlicz
\Psi_{2}
-process with respect to the metric
\rho
if

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{Z_{x}-Z_{x^{\prime}}}}}\right\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\rho(x,x^{\prime})\quad\text{for all}\quad x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T},

(224)

where for any random variable
Z
, its Orlicz
\Psi_{2}
-norm is defined as

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{Z}}}\right\|_{\Psi_{2}}=\inf\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\lambda>0:{\mathbb{E}}[\exp(Z^{2}/\lambda^{2})]\leq 2}}\right\}.

(225)

Our next result (see
Section

K.6
for the proof) establishes that
\psi_{n/2}
is an Orlicz process with respect to a suitable metric. (For clarity, we use
\mathcal{B}_{2}
to denote the Euclidean ball.)

Lemma 18

(
\psi_{n/2}
is an Orlicz
\Psi_{2}
-process)
.

If
\mathbf{k}
satisfies
1
and
2
, then, given any fixed
R>0
, the random process
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\psi_{n/2}(x),x\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(0;R)}}\right\}
is an Orlicz
\Psi_{2}
-process with respect to the metric
\rho
defined in
Eq.

227
, i.e.,

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\psi_{n/2}(x)-\psi_{n/2}(x^{\prime})}}}\right\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\rho(x,x^{\prime})\quad\text{for all}\quad x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(0;R),

(226)

where the metric
\rho
is defined as

\textstyle\rho(x,x^{\prime})=\sqrt{\frac{8}{3}}\cdot\sigma_{n/2}\cdot\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{2L_{\mathbf{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-x^{\prime}}}}\right\|_{2}},2\sqrt{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}}\right).

(227)

Given
Lemma

18
, we can invoke high probability bounds
for Orlicz processes. For the Orlicz process
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\psi_{n/2}(x),x\in\mathbb{T}}}\right\}
, given any fixed
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1]
,
Wainwright 2019
implies that

\textstyle\sup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\psi_{n/2}(x)-\psi_{n/2}(x^{\prime})}}\right|\leq 8\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(D)+D\sqrt{\log(4/\delta^{\prime})}}}\right),

(228)

with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}/2
, where

D\triangleq\sup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}}\rho(x,x^{\prime})
denotes the
\rho
-diameter of
\mathbb{T}
, and the quantity
\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(D)
is defined as

\textstyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(D)\triangleq\int_{0}^{D}\sqrt{\log(1+\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(u))}du.

(229)

Here
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(u)
denotes the
u
-covering number of
\mathbb{T}
with respect to metric
\rho
, namely the cardinality of the smallest cover
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(u)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\textstyle\mathbb{T}\subseteq\cup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(u)}\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(z;u)\quad\text{where}\quad\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(z;u)\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\rho(z,x)\leq u}}\right\}.

(230)

To avoid confusion, let
\mathcal{B}_{2}
denote the Euclidean ball (metric induced by
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\cdot}}}\right\|_{2}
). Then in our setting, we have
\mathbb{T}=\mathcal{B}_{2}(0;R)
and hence

\textstyle D_{R}\triangleq\sup_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(0;R)}\rho(x,x^{\prime})=\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{R}}{2}},\sigma_{n/2}\sqrt{\frac{32\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{3}}}}\right),\ \text{with}\ \beta_{R}=\frac{32}{3}\sigma^{2}_{n/2}L_{\mathbf{k}}R.

(231)

Some algebra establishes that this definition of
D_{R}
is identical to that specified in
Lemma

13
.
Next, we derive bounds for
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}
(see
Section

K.7
for the proof).

Lemma 19

(Bounds on
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}
)
.

If
\mathbf{k}
satisfies
1
and
2
, then, for
\mathbb{T}=\mathcal{B}_{2}(0;R)
with
R>0
and
\rho
defined in
Eq.

227
, we have

\textstyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(u)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\beta_{R}/{u^{2}}}}\right)^{d},\quad\text{and}\quad

(232)

\textstyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(D_{R})

\textstyle\leq\sqrt{d}D_{R}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[3+\sqrt{2\log({\beta_{R}}/{D_{R}^{2}})}}}\right],

(233)

where
D_{R}
and
\beta_{R}
are defined in
Eq.

231
.

Doing some algebra, we find that

\textstyle\frac{\beta_{R}}{D_{R}^{2}}=\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2,\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}R}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}}\right)\leq 2+\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}R}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}.

(234)

Moreover, note that
3+\sqrt{2\log(2+a)}\leq 6\sqrt{\log(2+a)}
for all
a\geq 0
, so that the bound
Eq.

233
can be simplified to

\textstyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(D_{R})\leq 6\sqrt{d}D_{R}\sqrt{\log(2+\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}R}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}})}.

(235)

Substituting the bound
Eq.

235
in
Eq.

228
yields that the event
\mathcal{E}_{\textup{sup}}
defined in
Eq.

156
holds with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}/2
, as claimed.

K.6
Proof of
Lemma

18
:
\psi_{n/2}
is an Orlicz
\Psi_{2}
-process

The proof of this lemma follows from the sub-Gaussianity of
\psi_{n/2}
established in
Theorem

3
. Introduce the shorthand
Y\triangleq\psi_{n/2}(x)-\psi_{n/2}(x^{\prime})
. Applying property
(i)
of
Theorem

3
with
u=\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)-\mathbf{k}(x^{\prime},\cdot)
along with the reproducing property of the kernel
\mathbf{k}
, we find that for any
t\in\real
,

\textstyle{\mathbb{E}}[\exp(tY)]={\mathbb{E}}[\exp(t\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\psi_{n/2},\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)\!-\!\mathbf{k}(x^{\prime},\cdot)}}\right\rangle_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}})]\leq\exp(\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\sigma_{n/2}^{2}\|{\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)\!-\!\mathbf{k}(x^{\prime},\cdot)}\|_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}^{2}).

(236)

That is, the random variable
Y
is sub-Gaussian with parameter
\sigma_{Y}\triangleq\sigma_{n/2}\|{\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)-\mathbf{k}(x^{\prime},\cdot)}\|_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}
. Next,
Wainwright 2019
yields that

{\mathbb{E}}[\exp(\frac{3Y^{2}}{8\sigma_{Y}^{2}})]\leq 2
, which in turn implies that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{Y}}}\right\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{8}{3}}\sigma_{Y}
.
Moreover, we have

\textstyle\|{\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)-\mathbf{k}(x^{\prime},\cdot)}\|_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}^{2}=\mathbf{k}(x,x)-\mathbf{k}(x,x^{\prime})+\mathbf{k}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime})-\mathbf{k}(x^{\prime},x)\leq\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2L_{\mathbf{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-x^{\prime}}}}\right\|_{2},4\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}\right),

(237)

using the Lipschitz continuity of
\mathbf{k}
and the definition of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbf{k}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

Eq.

5
.
Putting the pieces together along with the definition (
227
) of
\rho
, we find that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{Y}}}\right\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\rho(x,x^{\prime})
thereby yielding the claim (
226
).

K.7
Proof of
Lemma

19
: Bounds on
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}
and
