\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}

We use the relation of
\rho
to the Euclidean norm
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\cdot}}}\right\|_{2}
to establish the bound (
232
). The definitions
Eqs.

227
and
230
imply that

\textstyle\mathcal{B}_{2}(z;\frac{u^{2}}{\alpha})\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(z;u)\quad\text{for}\quad\alpha\triangleq\frac{16}{3}\sigma^{2}_{n/2}L_{\mathbf{k}},

(238)

since
\rho(x,x^{\prime})\leq\sqrt{\alpha\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-x^{\prime}}}}\right\|_{2}}
. Consequently, any
u^{2}/\alpha
-cover
\mathcal{C}
of
\mathbb{T}
in the Euclidean (
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\cdot}}}\right\|_{2}
) metric automatically yields a
u
-cover of
\mathbb{T}
in
\rho
metric as we note that

\textstyle\mathcal{B}_{2}(0;R)\subseteq\cup_{z\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{B}_{2}(z;\frac{u^{2}}{\alpha})\subseteq\cup_{z\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(z;u).

(239)

Consequently, the smallest cover
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(u)
would not be larger than than the smallest cover
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{T},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\cdot}}}\right\|_{2}}(u^{2}/\alpha)
, or equivalently:

\textstyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(u)\leq\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{T},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\cdot}}}\right\|_{2}}(\frac{u^{2}}{\alpha}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+{2R\alpha}/{u^{2}}}}\right)^{d},

(240)

where inequality (i) follows from
Wainwright 2019
using the fact that
\mathbb{T}=\mathcal{B}_{2}(0;R)
. Noting that
\beta_{R}=2R\alpha
yields the bound (
232
).

We now use the bound (
232
) on the covering number to establish the bound
Eq.

233
.

Proof of bound on
\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}

Applying the definition
Eq.

229
and the bound
Eq.

232
, we find that

\textstyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(D_{R})

\textstyle\leq\int_{0}^{D_{R}}\sqrt{\log(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+{\beta_{R}}/{u^{2}}}}\right)^{d})}du

(241)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{R}}{2}}\int_{{\beta_{R}}/{D_{R}^{2}}}^{\infty}s^{-3/2}\sqrt{\log(1+(1+s)^{d})}ds,

(242)

where step (i) follows from a change of variable
s\leftarrow\beta_{R}/u^{2}
.
Note that
\log(1+a)\leq\log a+1/a
, and
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
for any
a,b\geq 0
. Applying these inequalities, we find that

\textstyle\log(1+(1+s)^{d})\leq d\log(2+s)\leq d(\log s+\frac{1}{s}+\frac{1}{1+s})\leq d(\log s+\frac{2}{s}),

(243)

for
s\in[\beta_{R}/D_{R}^{2},\infty)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:max_by_sum}}}{{\subseteq}}[2,\infty)
. Consequently,

\textstyle\int_{\beta_{R}/D_{R}^{2}}^{\infty}s^{-3/2}\sqrt{\log(1+(1+s)^{d})}ds\leq\sqrt{d}\int_{\beta_{R}/D_{R}^{2}}^{\infty}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log s}}{s^{3/2}}+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{s^{2}}}}\right)ds.

(244)

Next, we note that

\textstyle\int_{\beta_{R}/D_{R}^{2}}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{s^{2}}ds

\textstyle=\sqrt{2}\frac{D_{R}^{2}}{\beta_{R}},\quad\text{and}\quad

(245)

\textstyle\int_{\beta_{R}/D_{R}^{2}}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{\log s}}{s^{3/2}}ds

\textstyle=-2\sqrt{\frac{\log s}{s}}\!-\!\sqrt{2}\Gamma(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\log s)\big|_{s=\beta_{R}/D_{R}^{2}}^{s=\infty}

(246)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\frac{2D_{R}}{\sqrt{\beta_{R}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\log(\beta_{R}/D_{R}^{2})}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log(\beta_{R}/D_{R}^{2})}}}}\right),

(247)

where step (i) follows from the following bound on the incomplete Gamma function:

\textstyle\Gamma(\frac{1}{2},a)=\int_{a}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-t}dt\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{a}}\int_{a}^{\infty}e^{-t}dt=a^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-a}\quad\text{for any}\quad a>0.

(248)

Putting the bounds
Eqs.

242
,
244
,
245
, and
247
together, we find that

\textstyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{T},\rho}(D_{R})\leq\sqrt{d}D_{R}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{D_{R}}{\sqrt{\beta_{R}}}+\sqrt{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\log(\beta_{R}/D_{R}^{2})}+1/{\sqrt{\log(\beta_{R}/D_{R}^{2})}}}}\right)}}\right).

(249)

Note that by definition
Eq.

231

\frac{D_{R}}{\sqrt{\beta_{R}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
. Using this observation and the fact that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\log 2}}\leq 3
yields the claimed bound
Eq.

233
.

Appendix L
Proof of
Remark

8
: Example settings for KH
L^{\infty}
rates

Note that when the kernel is restricted to a compact domain, we have
R_{\mathbf{k},n}\leq\mathcal{O}(\sigma)
so that in this case,
\frac{L_{\mathbf{k}}(R_{\mathbf{k},n}\wedge R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}})}{\|\mathbf{k}\|_{\infty}}\leq\mathcal{O}(L)
.
Next, we establish the bounds on
L
and
\kappa^{\dagger}(1/n)
for each of the kernels, which immediately imply the respective claims both when supported on
d
and on a compact domain with
\sigma=\sqrt{d}
.

•

For a Gaussian kernel, we have
\kappa(r)=e^{-r^{2}/2}
, which when put together with
Eqs.

279
and
280
implies that
L=\sqrt{2/e}
, and
\kappa^{\dagger}(1/n)=\sqrt{2\log n}
.

•

For a Matérn kernel, we have
\kappa(r)=c_{b}r^{b}K_{b}(r)
for
b=\nu-\frac{d}{2}
, for suitable constant
c_{b}
, and function
K_{b}
; see
Table

1
. Moreover,
Eqs.

292
and
293
imply that
L=\frac{C_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{2}+b}}\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{2}+1}}
for universal constants
C_{1},C_{2}
, and
\kappa^{\dagger}(1/n)\leq\mathcal{O}(\max(b\log b,\log n))=\mathcal{O}(\log n)
.

•

For a IMQ kernels, we have
\kappa(r)=\frac{1}{(\gamma^{2}+r^{2})^{\nu}}
, so that

\textstyle L\leq\sup_{r}\kappa^{\prime}(r)=\sup_{r}\frac{2\nu r}{(\gamma^{2}+r^{2})^{\nu+1}}=\frac{2\nu\gamma}{\gamma^{2(\nu+1)}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+2\nu}}(\frac{2\nu+1}{2\nu+2})^{\nu+1},

(250)

and
\kappa^{\dagger}(1/n)=\sqrt{n^{1/\nu}-\gamma^{2}}\leq n^{1/2\nu}
.

Appendix M
Proof of
Corollary

5
:
L^{\infty}
and MMD guarantees for
kt-split

First applying
Theorem

4
Item

(b)
with
\mathbf{k}={\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
yields that

\textstyle\|\mathbb{P}_{n}{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}-\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{(m,1)}{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(n,m,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n})

(251)

with probability at least
1\!-\!\delta^{\prime}\!-\!\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2^{j-1}}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m-j}\lfloor{n/2^{m}}\rfloor}\delta_{i}
. Call this event
\mathcal{E}
.

Let
r^{\prime}=R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\prime}+\varepsilon
denote a shorthand notation, where
\varepsilon>0
is an arbitrary scalar.
Applying
Theorem

2
with
r=2r^{\prime}
and
a=b=\frac{1}{2}
, we find that on event
\mathcal{E}
,

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathcal{S}^{(m,1)})

(252)

\textstyle=\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mathbb{P}_{n},\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{(m,1)})

(253)

\textstyle\leq\|\mathbb{P}_{n}{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}-\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{(m,1)}{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}e_{d}r^{d/2}+2\tau_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(r^{\prime})+2\|{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\!\cdot\max({\tau_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(r^{\prime}),\tau_{\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{(m,1)}}(r^{\prime})})

(254)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\|\mathbb{P}_{n}{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}-\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{(m,1)}{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(4\pi)^{d/2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}}(r^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}}+\frac{2\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}},

(255)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:ksqrt_linf_guarantee}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\cdot\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(n,m,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(4\pi)^{d/2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}}(r^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}}+\frac{2\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}

(256)

\textstyle=\frac{\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[2+\sqrt{\frac{(4\pi)^{d/2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}}(r^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(n,m,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n})}}\right]

(257)

where step (i) uses the following arguments: (a)
e_{d}r^{d/2}=\sqrt{\frac{(4\pi)^{d/2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}}(r^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}}
; (b)
\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{(m,1)}
is supported on a subset of points from
\mathcal{S}_{n}
and hence

\textstyle\max\{{\tau_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(r^{\prime}),\tau_{\mathbb{Q}_{\mathrm{KT}}^{(m,1)}}(r^{\prime})}\}=\tau_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(r^{\prime})=0\quad\text{for any }\quad r^{\prime}>R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}};

(258)

and (c)
\tau_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(r^{\prime})\leq\frac{\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}
for any
r^{\prime}>R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\prime}
.
Noting that
Eq.

257
applies simultaneously for all
\varepsilon>0
under event
\mathcal{E}
, taking the limit
\varepsilon\to 0
yields that

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathcal{S}^{(m,1)})\leq\frac{\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}}{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[2+\sqrt{\frac{(4\pi)^{d/2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}}(R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(n,m,d,\delta^{\star},\delta^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n},{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n})}}\right]

(259)

with probability at least
1\!-\!\delta^{\prime}\!-\!\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2^{j-1}}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m-j}\lfloor{n/2^{m}}\rfloor}\delta_{i}
, as claimed.

Appendix N
Derivation of
Table

1
: Square-root kernels
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
for common target kernels
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}

In this appendix, we derive the results stated in
Table

1
.

N.1
General proof strategy

Let
\mathcal{F}
denote the Fourier operator
Eq.

27
.
In
Table

3
, we state the continuous
\kappa
such that

{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,y)=\kappa(x-y)
in the first column, its Fourier transform
Eq.

27

\widehat{\kappa}
in the second column, the square-root Fourier transform in the third column, and the square-root kernel in the fourth column, given by

{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/4}}{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x-y)

with
{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}=\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{\widehat{\kappa}})
.

Proposition

3
along with expressions in
Table

3
directly establishes the validity of the square-root kernels for the Gaussian and (scaled) B-spline kernels. For completeness, we also illustrate the remaining calculus for the B-spline kernels in
Section

N.3
. We do a similar calculation in
Section

N.2
for the Matérn kernel for better exposition of the involved expressions.

for
\kappa(z)
Expression

transform of
\kappa
:

\widehat{\kappa}(\omega)
Expression for Fourier

transform:
\sqrt{\widehat{\kappa}(\omega)}
Square-root Fourier

{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(z)\triangleq(\frac{1}{2\pi})^{\frac{d}{4}}\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{\widehat{\kappa}})(z)
Expression for

\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\displaystyle\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}}\right)

\sigma^{d}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{2}}}\right)

\sigma^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{4}}}\right)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi\sigma^{2}}}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\displaystyle\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{d}\circledast^{2\beta+2}\mathbb{I}_{[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]}(z_{j})

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4^{\beta+1}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}}}\right)^{d}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sin^{2\beta+2}(\omega_{j}/2)}{\omega_{j}^{2\beta+2}}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4^{\beta+1}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\sin^{\beta+1}(\omega_{j}/2)}{\omega_{j}^{\beta+1}}

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{d}\circledast^{\beta+1}\mathbb{I}_{[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]}(z_{j})

Table 3:
Fourier transforms of kernels
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,y)=\kappa(x-y)
and square-root kernels
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)={\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x-y)
from
Table

1
.

Here
\mathcal{F}
denotes the Fourier operator (
27
), and

\circledast^{\ell}
denotes the convolution operator applied
\ell-1
times, with the convention
\circledast^{1}f\triangleq f
and
\circledast^{2}f=f\circledast f
for
(f\circledast g)(x)=\int f(y)g(x-y)dy
.
Each Fourier transform is derived from
Sriperumbudur et al. 2010
.

N.2
Deriving
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
for the Matérn kernel

For
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}\!=\!\textbf{Mat\'{e}rn }(\nu,\gamma)
from
Table

1
, we have

\textstyle{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,y)

\textstyle=\phi_{d,\nu,\gamma}\cdot\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}(x-y)\quad\text{where}\quad\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}(z)\triangleq c_{\nu}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}}{\gamma}}}\right)^{\nu-\frac{d}{2}}{K}_{\nu-\frac{d}{2}}(\gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}),

(260)

\textstyle\phi_{d,\nu,\gamma}

\textstyle\triangleq\frac{c_{\nu-d/2}}{c_{\nu}}\gamma^{2\nu-d}\quad\text{and}\quad c_{\nu}\triangleq\frac{2^{1-\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)},

(261)

and
{K}_{a}
denotes the modified Bessel function of third kind of order
a

(
Wendland 2004
, Def. 5.10)
. That is, for Matérn kernel with
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,y)=\kappa(x-y)
, the function
\kappa
is given by
\kappa=\phi_{d,\nu,\gamma}\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}
. Now applying
(
Wendland 2004
, Thm. is 8.15)
, we find that

\textstyle\mathcal{F}(\Phi_{\nu,\gamma})=\frac{1}{(\gamma^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2})^{\nu}}\quad{\Longrightarrow}\quad\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{\mathcal{F}(\Phi_{\nu,\gamma})})=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/4}}\Phi_{\nu/2,\gamma},

(262)

where in the last step we also use the facts that
\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}
is an even function and
\sqrt{\mathcal{F}(\Phi_{\nu,\gamma})}\in{L}^{1}
for all
\nu>d/2
.
Thus, by
Proposition

3
, a valid square-root kernel
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)={\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x-y)
is defined via

\textstyle{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}=\sqrt{\phi_{d,\nu,\gamma}}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/4}}\Phi_{\nu/2,\gamma},\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}

\textstyle=A_{\nu,\gamma,d}\cdot\textbf{Mat\'{e}rn }(\nu/2,\gamma),

(263)

\textstyle\quad\text{where}\quad A_{\nu,\gamma,d}

\textstyle\triangleq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{4\pi}\gamma^{2}}}\right)^{d/4}\sqrt{\frac{\Gamma(\nu)}{\Gamma(\nu-d/2)}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma((\nu-d)/2)}{\Gamma(\nu/2)}.

(264)

N.3
Deriving
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
for the B-Spline kernel

For positive integers
\beta,d
, define the constants

\textstyle\mathfrak{B}_{\beta}\triangleq\frac{1}{(\beta-1)!}\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\beta/2\rfloor}(-1)^{j}{\beta\choose j}(\frac{\beta}{2}-j)^{\beta-1},\qquad S_{\beta,d}\triangleq\mathfrak{B}_{\beta}^{-d},\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{S}_{\beta,d}\triangleq\frac{\sqrt{S_{2\beta+2,d}}}{S_{\beta+1,d}}.

(265)

Define the function
\chi_{\beta}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\real
as follows:

\textstyle\chi_{\beta}(z)\triangleq S_{\beta,d}\prod_{i=1}^{d}f_{\beta}(z_{i}).

(266)

Then for kernel
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}=\textbf{B-spline}(2\beta+1)
, we have

\textstyle{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,y)=\chi_{2\beta+2}(x-y).

(267)

The second row of
Table

3
implies that

\textstyle(\frac{1}{2\pi})^{\frac{d}{4}}\mathcal{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{F}(\chi_{2\beta+2})}}}\right)=\frac{\sqrt{S_{2\beta+2,d}}}{S_{\beta+1,d}}\cdot\chi_{\beta+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:spline_all_constants}}}{{=}}\widetilde{S}_{\beta,d}\cdot\chi_{\beta+1},

(268)

where we also use the fact that
\chi_{\beta}
is an even function.
Putting the pieces together with
Proposition

3
, we conclude that

\textstyle{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}=\widetilde{S}_{\beta,d}\cdot\textbf{B-spline}(\beta),

(269)

(with
{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}=\widetilde{S}_{\beta,d}\chi_{\beta+1}
)
is a valid square-root kernel for
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}=\textbf{B-spline}(2\beta+1)
.

Appendix O
Derivation of
Table

2
: Explicit bounds on
Theorem

1
quantities for common kernels

We start by collecting some common tools in
Section

O.1
that we later use for our derivations for the Gaussian, Matérn and B-spline kernels in
Sections

O.2
,
O.3
, and
O.4
respectively. Finally, in
Section

O.5
, we put the pieces together to derive explicit MMD rates, as a function of
d,n,\delta
and kernel parameters for the three kernels, and summarize the rates in
Table

4
. (This table is complementary to the generic results stated in
Corollary

1
.)

O.1
Common tools for our derivations

We collect some simplified expressions, and techniques that come handy in our derivations to follow.

Simplified bounds on
\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}

From
Eq.

8
, we have

\textstyle\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(n,\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}n,d,\frac{\delta}{n},\delta^{\prime},R)

\textstyle\precsim\sqrt{\log\frac{n}{\delta}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log(\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}})+d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}\cdot(R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}+R)}}\right)}}\right]}

(270)

\textstyle\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(n,\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}n,d,\frac{\delta}{n},\delta,R)

\textstyle\precsim_{\delta}\sqrt{d\log n\cdot\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}\cdot(R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}+R)}}\right)}.

(271)

Thus, given
Eq.

271
, to get a bound on
\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}
, we need to derive bounds on
(L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty},R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n})
for various kernels to obtain the desired guarantees on MMD and
L^{\infty}
coresets.

Bounds on Gamma function

Our proofs make use of the bounds from
Batir 2017
on the Gamma function:

\textstyle\Gamma(b+1)\geq(b/e)^{b}\sqrt{2\pi b}\text{ for any }b\geq 1,\text{ and }\Gamma(b+1)\leq(b/e)^{b}\sqrt{e^{2}b}\text{ for any }b\geq 1.1.

(272)

General tools for bounding the Lipschitz constant

To bound the Lipschitz constant
L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}
, the following two observations come in handy. For a radial kernel
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)=\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-y}}}\right\|_{2})
with
\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}:\real_{+}\to\real
, we note

\textstyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y,z:\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y-z}}}\right\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)-{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,z)}}\right|

\textstyle\leq\sup_{a>0,b\leq r}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(a)-\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(a+b)}}\right|\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}^{\prime}}}}\right\|_{\infty}r

(273)

\textstyle\quad\Longrightarrow\quad L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}^{\prime}}}}\right\|_{\infty}.

(274)

For a translation-invariant kernel
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)={\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x-y)
with
{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\real
, we use the bound

\textstyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y,z:\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{y-z}}}\right\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)-{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,z)}}\right|

\textstyle=\sup_{z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z-z^{\prime}}}}\right\|_{2}\leq r}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(z^{\prime})-{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(z^{\prime\prime})}}\right|

(275)

\textstyle\leq\sup_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\nabla{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(z^{\prime})}}}\right\|_{2}r,

(276)

\textstyle\quad\Longrightarrow\quad L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}

\textstyle\leq\sup_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\nabla{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(z^{\prime})}}}\right\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{d}\sup_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\nabla{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(z^{\prime})}}}\right\|_{\infty},

(277)

where step (i) follows from Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality (and is handy when coordinate wise control on
\nabla{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}
is easier to derive).
We later apply the inequality
Eq.

274
for the Gaussian and Matérn kernels, and
Eq.

277
for the B-spline kernel.

O.2
Proofs for Gaussian kernel

For the kernel
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}=\textbf{Gauss}(\sigma)
and its square-root kernel
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{\pi\sigma^{2}}}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}\textbf{Gauss}(\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{2}})
, we claim the following bounds on various quantities

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

\textstyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{\pi\sigma^{2}}}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}=1,

(278)

\textstyle L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{\pi\sigma^{2}}}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}\frac{\sqrt{2/e}}{\sigma}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}/{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:gauss_kinf}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sigma}{\sqrt{2/e}}

(279)

\textstyle R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}

\textstyle=\sigma\sqrt{\log n},

(280)

\textstyle R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime}

\textstyle=\mathcal{O}(\sigma\sqrt{d+\log n}).

(281)

Substituting these expressions in
Eq.

271
, we find that

\textstyle\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}^{\textbf{Gauss}}(n,\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}n,d,\frac{\delta}{2n},\delta^{\prime},R)

\textstyle\precsim\sqrt{\log(\frac{n}{\delta})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}+d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\log n}+\frac{R}{\sigma}}}\right)}}\right]},

(282)

as claimed in
Table

2
.

Proof of claims
Eqs.

278
,
279
,
280
, and
281

The claim
Eq.

278
follows directly from the definition of
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
. The bound
Eq.

279
on
L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}
follows from the fact
\|{\frac{d}{dr}e^{-r^{2}/\sigma^{2}}}\|_{\infty}=\frac{\sqrt{2/e}}{\sigma}
and invoking the relation
Eq.

274
. Next, recalling the definition
Definition

7
and noting that

\textstyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y:\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-y}}}\right\|_{2}\geq r\end{subarray}}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)}}\right|\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{\pi\sigma^{2}}}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}e^{-r^{2}/\sigma^{2}}

(283)

implies the bound
Eq.

280
on
R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}
.

Next, we have

\textstyle\tau_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}^{2}(R)

\textstyle=\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}\geq R}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{\pi\sigma^{2}}}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp(-2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}/\sigma^{2})dz

(284)

\textstyle=\mathrm{Pr}_{X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}/4\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d})}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{X}}}\right\|_{2}\geq R)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}e^{-R^{2}/\sigma^{2}},

(285)

for
R\geq\sigma\sqrt{2d}
, where step (i) follows from the standard tail bound for a chi-squared random variable
Y
with
k
degree of freedom
(
Laurent and Massart 2000
, Lem. 1)
:
\mathrm{Pr}(Y-k>2\sqrt{kt}+2t)\leq e^{-t}
, wherein we substitute
k=d
,
Y=\frac{4}{\sigma^{2}}\|X\|_{2}^{2}
,
t=d\alpha
with
\alpha\geq 2
, and
R^{2}=\sigma^{2}t
. Using the tail bound
Eq.

285
, the bound
Eq.

278
on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}
and the definition
Definition

7
of
R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime}
, we find that

\textstyle R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}(\sigma\max(\sqrt{(\log n-\frac{d}{2}\log(\sigma{\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}}))_{+}},\sqrt{d}))=\mathcal{O}(\sigma\sqrt{d+\log n}).

(286)

yielding the claim
Eq.

281
.

O.3
Proofs for Matérn kernel

Recall the notations from
Eqs.

260
,
263
, and
264
for the Matérn kernel related quantities. Let
{K}_{b}
denote the modified
Bessel function of the third kind with order
b

(
Wendland 2004
, Def. 5.10)
. Let
a\triangleq\frac{\nu-d}{2}
. Then, the kernel
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}=\textbf{Mat\'{e}rn }(\nu,\gamma)
and its square-root kernel
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}=A_{\nu,\gamma,d}\cdot\textbf{Mat\'{e}rn }(a,\gamma)
satisfy

\textstyle{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,y)

\textstyle=\widetilde{\kappa}_{\nu-d/2}(\gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-y}}}\right\|_{2}),\quad\text{and}\quad

(287)

\textstyle{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)

\textstyle=A_{\nu,\gamma,d}\widetilde{\kappa}_{a}(\gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-y}}}\right\|_{2}),

(288)

\textstyle\text{ where }\widetilde{\kappa}_{b}(r)

\textstyle\triangleq c_{b}r^{b}{K}_{b}(r),\text{ and }c_{b}=\frac{2^{1-b}}{\Gamma(b)}\ \text{for}\ b>0.

(289)

We claim the following bounds on various quantities assuming
a\geq 2.2
, and
d\geq 2
:
8
8

8

When
a\in(1,2.2)
, and
d\in[1,2)
, analogous bounds with slightly different constants follow from employing the Gamma function upper bound of
Batir 2017
in place of our upper bound
Eq.

272
. For brevity, we omit these derivations.

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

\textstyle=A_{\nu,\gamma,d}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}=1,

(290)

\textstyle A_{\nu,\gamma,d}

\textstyle\leq 5\nu(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2\pi(a-1)})^{\frac{d}{4}},

(291)

\textstyle L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}

\textstyle\leq A_{\nu,\gamma,d}\frac{C_{1}\gamma}{\sqrt{a+C_{2}}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:collect_matern_rt_kinf}}}{{\leq}}\frac{C_{1}\gamma}{\sqrt{a+C_{2}}},

(292)

\textstyle R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}

\textstyle=\frac{1}{\gamma}\mathcal{O}(\max(\log n-a\log(1+a),C_{2}a\log(1+a))),

(293)

\textstyle R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime}

\textstyle=\frac{1}{\gamma}\cdot\mathcal{O}(a\!+\!\log n\!+\!d\log(\frac{\sqrt{2e\pi}}{\gamma})\!+\!\log(\frac{(\nu-2)^{\nu-\frac{3}{2}}}{(2(a-1))^{2a-1}d^{\frac{d}{2}+1}}))

(294)

We prove these claims in
Section

O.3.1
through
Section

O.3.5
. Putting these bounds together with
Eq.

271
yields that

\textstyle\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}^{\textbf{Mat\'{e}rn }}(n,\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}n,d,\frac{\delta}{2n},\delta^{\prime},R)

\textstyle\precsim\begin{cases}\sqrt{\log(\frac{n}{\delta})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\frac{1}{\delta}+d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+a}}\cdot(\log n\!+\!\gamma R)\!}}\right)}}\right]}\ \text{ if }a=o(\log n)\\
\sqrt{\log(\frac{n}{\delta})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}+d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{a}\log(1+a)\!+\!\gamma R\!}}\right)}}\right]}\ \text{ if }a=\Omega(\log n)\end{cases}

(295)

\textstyle\precsim\sqrt{\log(\frac{n}{\delta})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}+d\log(\log n\!+\!B+\!\gamma R)\!}}\right]}

(296)

with
B=a\log(1+a)
,
as claimed in
Table

2
.

O.3.1
Set-up for proofs of Matérn kernel quantities

Before proceeding to the proofs of the claims
Eqs.

290
,
291
,
292
,
293
, and
294
,
we collect some handy inequalities. Applying
Wendland 2004
,
we have

\textstyle\widetilde{\kappa}_{a}(\gamma r)

\textstyle\leq\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1,\sqrt{2\pi}c_{a}(\gamma r)^{a-\frac{1}{2}}e^{{-\gamma r+\frac{a^{2}}{2\gamma r}}}}}\right)\quad\text{for}\quad r>0.

(297)

For a large enough
r
, we also establish the following bound:

\textstyle\widetilde{\kappa}_{a}(\gamma r)

\textstyle\leq\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1,4c_{a}(\gamma r)^{a-1}e^{-\gamma r/2}}}\right)\quad\text{for}\quad\gamma r\geq 2(a-1),a\geq 1.

(298)

Proof of
Eq.

298

Noting the definition
Eq.

289
of
\kappa_{a}
, it suffices to show the following bound:

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{K}_{a}(r)}}\right|\leq\frac{4}{r}e^{-r/2}\quad\text{for}\quad r/2\geq a-1,

(299)

where
{K}_{a}
is the modified Bessel function of the third kind.
Using
Wendland 2004
, we have

\textstyle{K}_{a}(r)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-r\cosh t}\cosh(at)dt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{r}{2}e^{t}}e^{at}dt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}\int_{r/2}^{\infty}e^{-s}(\frac{2s}{r})^{a}\cdot\frac{1}{s}ds\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}\frac{4}{r}e^{-r/2}

(300)

where step (i) uses the following inequalities:
\cosh t\triangleq\frac{1}{2}(e^{t}+e^{-t})\geq\frac{1}{2}e^{t}
and
\cosh(at)\leq e^{at}
for
a>0,t>0
, step (ii) follows from a change of variable
s\leftarrow\frac{r}{2}e^{t}
, and finally step (iii) uses the following bound on the incomplete Gamma function obtained by substituting
B=2
and
A=a
in
Borwein and Chan 2009
:

\textstyle\int_{r}^{\infty}t^{a-1}e^{-t}dt\leq 2r^{a-1}e^{-r}\quad\text{for}\quad r\geq a-1.

(301)

The proof is now complete.

O.3.2
Proof of the bound
Eq.

290
on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

We claim that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\widetilde{\kappa}_{b}}}}\right\|_{\infty}=1\quad\text{for all}\quad b>0.

(302)

where was defined in
Eq.

289
. Putting the equality
Eq.

302

with
Eqs.

287
,
288
, and
297

immediately implies the bounds
Eq.

290
on the

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}
.
To prove
Eq.

302
, we follow the steps from the proof
of
Wendland 2004
.
Using
Wendland 2004
, for
b>0
we have

\textstyle{K}_{b}(r)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-r\cosh t}e^{bt}dt=\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{r}{2}(e^{t}+e^{-t})}e^{bt}dt=k^{-b}\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-r/2(u/k+k/u)}u^{b-1}du

(303)

where the last step follows by substituting
u=ke^{t}
. Setting
k=r/2
, we
find that

\textstyle r^{b}{K}_{b}(r)=2^{b-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-u}e^{-r^{2}/(4u)}u^{b-1}du\leq 2^{b-1}\Gamma(b),

(304)

where we achieve equality in the last step when we take the limit
r\to 0
. Noting that

\widetilde{\kappa}_{b}(r)=\frac{2^{1-b}}{\Gamma(b)}r^{b}{K}_{b}(r)

Eq.

289
yields the claim
Eq.

302
.

O.3.3
Proof of the bound
Eq.

291

on
A_{\nu,\gamma,d}

Using the definition
Eq.

264
we have
A_{\nu,\gamma,d}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{4\pi}\gamma^{2}}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}\cdot A_{\nu,\gamma,d}^{\prime}
,
where

\textstyle A_{\nu,\gamma,d}^{\prime}

\textstyle=\sqrt{\frac{\Gamma(\nu)}{\Gamma(\nu-d/2)}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma((\nu-d)/2)}{\Gamma(\nu/2)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:stirling_approx}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{\frac{e}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{(\frac{\nu-1}{e})^{\nu-1}\sqrt{\nu-1}}{(\frac{\nu-\frac{d}{2}-1}{e})^{\nu-\frac{d}{2}-1}\sqrt{\nu-\frac{d}{2}-1}}}\cdot\frac{e}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{(\frac{\nu-d-2}{2e})^{\frac{\nu-d-2}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\nu-d-2}{2}}}{(\frac{\nu-2}{2e})^{\frac{\nu-2}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\nu-2}{2}}}

(305)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{e^{2}}{2\pi}}}\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}(2\sqrt{e})^{\frac{d}{2}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\nu-1}{\nu-2}}}\right)^{\frac{\nu-2}{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\nu-1}{\nu-2}}\cdot(\nu-1)^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\nu-d-2}{\nu-\frac{d}{2}-1}}}\right)^{\frac{\nu-\frac{d}{2}-2}{2}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{(\nu-d-2)/\sqrt{(\nu-\frac{d}{2}-1)}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\nu-d-2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{d}{2}-1)}}

(306)

\textstyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{e^{2}}{2\pi}}}\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}(2\sqrt{e})^{\frac{d}{2}}\cdot\sqrt{e}\cdot\sqrt{2}\cdot(\nu-1)^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot(\sqrt{e})^{-\frac{d}{2}+1}\cdot\frac{(\nu-d-2)/(\nu-\frac{d}{2}-1)^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\nu-d-2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}}

(307)

\textstyle\leq\frac{e^{2}\sqrt{2e}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{4}}}(\nu-1)^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot(\nu-\frac{d}{2}-1)^{\frac{3}{4}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\nu-d-2}{4}}}\right)^{-\frac{d}{4}}.

(308)

As a result, we have

\textstyle A_{\nu,\gamma,d}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{4\pi}\gamma^{2}}}\right)^{d/4}\cdot A_{\nu,\gamma,d}^{\prime}\leq 5\nu(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\pi(\nu-d-2)})^{\frac{d}{4}}=5\nu(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2\pi(a-1)})^{\frac{d}{4}},

(309)

as claimed.

O.3.4
Proof of the bound
Eq.

292
on
L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}

To derive a bound on the Lipschitz constant
L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}
, we bound the derivative of
\widetilde{\kappa}_{a}
. Using
(r^{a}{K}_{a}(r))^{\prime}=-r^{a}{K}_{a-1}(r)

(
Wendland 2004
, Eq. 5.2)
, we find that

\textstyle(\widetilde{\kappa}_{a}(\gamma r))^{\prime}=c_{a}\frac{d}{dr}((\gamma r)^{a}{K}_{a}(\gamma r))=-\frac{c_{a}}{c_{a-1}}\gamma^{2}r\widetilde{\kappa}_{a-1}(\gamma r).

(310)

Using
Eq.

297
, we have

\textstyle\frac{c_{a}}{c_{a-1}}\gamma^{2}r\widetilde{\kappa}_{a-1}(\gamma r)\leq\frac{c_{a}\gamma}{c_{a-1}}\min(\gamma r,\sqrt{2\pi}c_{a-1}(\gamma r)^{a-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\gamma r+\frac{(a-1)^{2}}{2\gamma r}})\quad\text{for}\quad r>0.

(311)

And
Eq.

298
implies that

\textstyle\frac{c_{a}}{c_{a-1}}\gamma^{2}r\widetilde{\kappa}_{a-1}(\gamma r)\leq\frac{c_{a}\gamma}{c_{a-1}}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\gamma r,4c_{a-1}(\gamma r)^{a-1}e^{-\gamma r/2}}}\right)\quad\text{for}\quad r\geq 2(a-2),a\geq 2.

(312)

Next, we make use of the following observations: The function
t^{b}e^{-t/2}
achieves its maximum at
t=2b
. Then for any function
f:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
f(t)\leq t
for all
t\geq 0
and
f(t)\leq\min(t,Ct^{b}e^{-t/2})
for
t>t^{\dagger}
with
t^{\dagger}<2b
, we have

\textstyle\sup_{t\geq 0}f(t)\leq\min(2b,C(2b)^{b}e^{-b}).

(313)

As a result, we conclude that

\textstyle\sup_{r\geq 0}\frac{c_{a}}{c_{a-1}}\gamma^{2}r\widetilde{\kappa}_{a-1}(\gamma r)\leq\frac{c_{a}\gamma}{c_{a-1}}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2(a-1),4c_{a-1}(2(a-1))^{a-1}e^{-a+1}}}\right).

(314)

Substituting the value of
c_{a-1}
and the bound
Eq.

272
, we can bound the second argument inside the min on the RHS of
Eq.

314
(for
a\geq 3
) as follows:

\textstyle 4c_{a-1}(2(a-1))^{a-1}e^{-a+1}

\textstyle\leq 4\cdot 2^{2-a}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(a-2)}}(\frac{e}{a-2})^{a-2}2^{a-1}(a-1)^{a-1}e^{1-a}

(315)

\textstyle\leq\frac{8}{e\sqrt{2\pi}}(1+\frac{1}{a-2})^{a-2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{a-2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{a-2}}}}\right)\leq\frac{8}{\sqrt{\pi}}\sqrt{a-2}

(316)

for
a\geq 3
. When
a\in[2,3)
, one can directly show that

4c_{a-1}(2(a-1))^{a-1}e^{-a+1}\leq 8/e
.
Putting the pieces together, and noting that
\frac{c_{a}}{c_{a-1}}=\frac{1}{\max(2(a-1),1)}
, we find that

\textstyle\sup_{r\geq 0}\frac{c_{a}}{c_{a-1}}\gamma^{2}r\widetilde{\kappa}_{a-1}(\gamma r)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\gamma}{\max(2(a-1),1)}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2(a-1),\frac{8}{e}+\frac{8}{\sqrt{\pi}}\sqrt{a-2}}}\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}\gamma}{\sqrt{a+C_{2}}}

(317)

for any
a\geq 2
. And hence

\textstyle L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}\leq A_{\nu,\gamma,d}\sup_{r\geq 0}|\kappa_{a}^{\prime}(\gamma r)|\leq A_{\nu,\gamma,d}\frac{C_{1}\gamma}{\sqrt{a+C_{2}}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:collect_matern_rt_kinf}}}{{\leq}}\frac{C_{1}\gamma}{\sqrt{a+C_{2}}},

(318)

as claimed.

O.3.5
Proof of the bound
Eq.

293
on
R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}

Using arguments similar to those used to obtain
Eq.

315
, we find that

\textstyle\max_{\gamma r\geq 2(a-1)}\widetilde{\kappa}_{a}(\gamma r)\leq 4c_{a}(2(a-1))^{a-1}e^{-(a-1)}\leq\sqrt{\frac{4}{a-1}}=\sqrt{\frac{8}{\nu-d-2}}.

(319)

Next, note that

\textstyle(\gamma r)^{a-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\gamma r+\frac{a^{2}}{2\gamma r}}

\textstyle\precsim e^{-\gamma r/2}\quad\text{for}\quad\gamma r\succsim a\log(1+a),

(320)

\textstyle c_{a}

\textstyle=\frac{2^{1-a}}{\Gamma(a)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:stirling_approx}}}{{\leq}}(\frac{2e}{a-1})^{a-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(a-1)}},

(321)

where
Eq.

320
follows from standard algebra.
Thus, we have

\textstyle c_{a}\widetilde{\kappa}_{a}(\gamma r)

\textstyle\precsim c_{a}\exp(-\gamma r/2)\quad\text{for}\quad\gamma r\succsim a\log(1+a)

(322)

\textstyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:stirling_approx}}}{{\Longrightarrow}}\quad R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}

\textstyle\precsim\frac{1}{\gamma}\max(\log n-a\log(1+a),C_{1}a\log(1+a)),

(323)

as claimed.

O.3.6
Proof of the bound
Eq.

294
on

R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime}

Let
V_{d}=\pi^{d/2}/\Gamma(d/2+1)
denote the volume of unit Euclidean ball in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Using
Eq.

288
, we have

\textstyle\frac{1}{A_{\nu,\gamma,d}^{2}}\tau_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}^{2}(R)=\int_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}\geq R}\widetilde{\kappa}_{a}^{2}(\gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2})dz

\textstyle=dV_{d}\int_{r>R}r^{d-1}\widetilde{\kappa}_{a}^{2}(\gamma r)dr

(324)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:matern_basic_bound}}}{{\leq}}2\pi c_{a}^{2}dV_{d}\int_{r>R}r^{d-1}(\gamma r)^{2a-1}\exp({-2\gamma r+\frac{a^{2}}{\gamma r}})dr

(325)

\textstyle=2\pi c_{a}^{2}dV_{d}\gamma^{1-d}\int_{r>R}(\gamma r)^{\nu-2}\exp({-2\gamma r+\frac{a^{2}}{\gamma r}})dr

(326)

where we have also used the fact that
2a+d=\nu
. Next, we note that

\textstyle\exp(-2\gamma r+\frac{a^{2}}{\gamma r})\leq\exp(-\frac{3}{2}\gamma r)\quad\text{for}\quad\gamma r\geq\sqrt{2}a

(327)

For any integer
b>1
, noticing that
f(t)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(b)}t^{b-1}e^{-t}
is the density function of an Erlang distribution with shape parameter
b
and rate parameter
1
, and using the expression for its complementary cumulative distribution function, we find that

\textstyle\int_{r}^{\infty}t^{b-1}e^{-t}dt=\Gamma(b)\sum_{i=0}^{b-1}\frac{r^{i}}{i!}e^{-r}

(328)

Thus, for
R>\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\gamma}a
, we have

\textstyle\int_{r>R}(\gamma r)^{\nu-2}\exp({-2\gamma r+\frac{a^{2}}{\gamma r}})dr

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:exp_bound_simplified}}}{{\leq}}\int_{r>R}(\gamma r)^{\nu-2}\exp({-\frac{3}{2}\gamma r})dr

(329)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{3}}}\right)^{\nu-1}\int_{3\gamma R/2}^{\infty}t^{\nu-2}e^{-t}dt

(330)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:IGF_formula}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{3}}}\right)^{\nu-1}\Gamma(\nu-1)e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma R}\sum_{i=0}^{\nu-1}\frac{(3\gamma R/2)^{i}}{i!}

(331)

\textstyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\Gamma(\nu-1)e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma R}e^{\gamma R}\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\Gamma(\nu-1)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma R}.

(332)

Putting the bounds
Eqs.

326
and
332
together for
R\geq\frac{\nu-d}{\sqrt{2}\gamma}
, we find that

\textstyle\tau_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}^{2}(R)

\textstyle\leq A_{\nu,\gamma,d}^{2}\cdot\gamma^{-d}\cdot 2\pi\frac{2^{2-2a}}{\Gamma^{2}(a)}\cdot\frac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}\cdot\Gamma(\nu-1)\exp(-\frac{1}{2}\gamma R).

(333)

Using
Eq.

272
, we have

\textstyle 2\pi\frac{2^{2-2a}}{\Gamma^{2}(a)}\cdot\frac{\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}\cdot\Gamma(\nu-1)

\textstyle\leq 2\pi\cdot\frac{2^{2-2a}e^{2(a-1)}}{2\pi(a-1)(a-1)^{2(a-1)}}\cdot\frac{\pi^{d/2}(2e)^{d/2}}{\sqrt{\pi d}\cdot d^{d/2}}\cdot e\sqrt{\nu-2}(\frac{\nu-2}{e})^{\nu-2}

(334)

\textstyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}}e^{2a-2+d/2+1-\nu}(2\pi)^{d/2}(2a-2)^{-(2a-1)}d^{-d/2-1}(\nu-2)^{\nu-3/2}

(335)

\textstyle=\frac{2}{e\sqrt{\pi}}(2e\pi)^{d/2}(\nu-d-2)^{-(\nu-d-1)}d^{-d/2-1}(\nu-2)^{\nu-3/2}

(336)

\textstyle=\frac{2}{e\sqrt{\pi}}(2e\pi)^{d/2}(2(a-1))^{-(2a-1)}d^{-d/2-1}(\nu-2)^{\nu-3/2}.

(337)

Putting the pieces together, and solving for
\tau_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(R)\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:collect_matern_rt_kinf}}}{{=}}A_{\nu,\gamma,d}/\sqrt{n}
, we find that
R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime}

Definition

7
can be bounded as

\textstyle R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime}

\textstyle\leq\frac{2}{\gamma}\cdot\max(\frac{a}{\sqrt{2}},\log n\!+\!\log(\frac{2}{e\sqrt{\pi}})\!+\!d\log(\frac{\sqrt{2e\pi}}{\gamma})\!+\!\log(\frac{(\nu-2)^{\nu-\frac{3}{2}}}{(2(a-1))^{2a-1}d^{\frac{d}{2}+1}}))

(338)

\textstyle\precsim\frac{1}{\gamma}\cdot(a\!+\!\log n\!+\!d\log(\frac{\sqrt{2e\pi}}{\gamma})\!+\!\log(\frac{(\nu-2)^{\nu-\frac{3}{2}}}{(2(a-1))^{2a-1}d^{\frac{d}{2}+1}}),

(339)

as claimed.

O.4
Proofs for B-spline kernel

Recall the notation from
Eqs.

265
,
266
,
267
, and
269
. Then, the kernel
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}=\textbf{B-spline}(2\beta+1)
and its square-root kernel
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}=\widetilde{S}_{\beta,d}\cdot\textbf{B-spline}(\beta)
satisfy

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

\textstyle=\widetilde{S}_{\beta,d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}c_{\beta}^{d}\quad\text{where}\quad c_{\beta}\triangleq\frac{\mathfrak{B}_{\beta+1}}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{B}_{2\beta+2}}}\begin{cases}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\text{ when }\beta=1\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}1\text{ when }\beta>1\end{cases},

(340)

\textstyle\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}

\textstyle=1,

(341)

\textstyle\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}

\textstyle\leq\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{d},

(342)

\textstyle R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}

\textstyle\leq\sqrt{d}(\beta+1)/2,\quad\text{and}\quad R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime}\leq\sqrt{d}(\beta+1)/2.

(343)

While claims (i) and (ii) follow directly from the definitions in the display
Eq.

265
, claim (iii) can be verified numerically, e.g., using SciPy
(
Virtanen et al. 2020
)
.
From numerical verification, we also find that the constant
c_{\beta}
in
Eq.

340
is decreasing with
\beta
.
See
Section

O.4.1
for the proofs of the remaining claims.
Finally, substituting various quantities from
Eqs.

340
,
342
, and
343
in
Eq.

271
, we find that

\textstyle\mathfrak{M}_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}^{\textbf{B-spline}}(n,\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}n,d,\frac{\delta}{2n},\delta^{\prime},R)

\textstyle\precsim\sqrt{\log(\frac{n}{\delta})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+d\log(d\beta+\sqrt{d}R)}}\right)}.

(344)

O.4.1
Proofs of the bounds on B-spline kernel quantities

We start with some basic set-up.
Consider the (unnormalized) univariate B-splines

\textstyle f_{\beta}:\real\to[0,1]\quad\text{with}\quad f_{\beta}(a)=\circledast^{\beta}\mathbb{I}_{[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]}(a)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{(\beta-1)!}\sum_{j=0}^{\beta}(-1)^{j}{\beta\choose j}(a+\frac{\beta}{2}-j)_{+}^{\beta-1}

(345)

where step (i) follows from
Schumaker 2007
. Noting that
f_{\beta}
is an even function with a unique global maxima at 0 (see
Schumaker 2007
for more details), we find that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f_{\beta}}}}\right\|_{\infty}=f_{\beta}(0)=\frac{1}{(\beta-1)!}\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\beta/2\rfloor}(-1)^{j}{\beta\choose j}(\frac{\beta}{2}-j)^{\beta-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:spline_all_constants}}}{{=}}\mathfrak{B}_{\beta}.

(346)

Bounds on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}
and
\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}

Recalling
Eqs.

266
and
267
, we find that

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\chi_{2\beta+2}}}}\right\|_{\infty}=S_{2\beta+2,d}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f_{2\beta+2}}}}\right\|_{\infty}^{d}=S_{2\beta+2,d}\mathfrak{B}_{2\beta+2}^{d}=1,

(347)

thereby establishing
Eq.

341
.

Bounds on
L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}

We have

\textstyle f^{\prime}_{\beta+1}(a)=\int f_{\beta}(b)\frac{d}{da}\mathbb{I}_{[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]}(a-b)dy=f_{\beta}(a-\frac{1}{2})-f_{\beta}(a+\frac{1}{2})

(348)

and hence
\|f^{\prime}_{\beta+1}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{a}|f_{\beta}(a-\frac{1}{2})-f_{\beta}(a+\frac{1}{2})|\leq\|f_{\beta}\|_{\infty}
since
f_{\beta}
is non-negative.
Putting the pieces together, we have

\textstyle\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}{\|{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\|_{\infty}}=\frac{1}{\widetilde{S}_{\beta,d}}L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}\leq\sup_{z}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\nabla\chi_{\beta+1}(z)}}}\right\|_{2}

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:splinefun}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{d}\cdot S_{\beta+1,d}\|f_{\beta+1}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\cdot\|f_{\beta+1}\|_{\infty}^{d-1}

(349)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:spline_all_constants},\ref{eq:unisplineinf})}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{d}\cdot\mathfrak{B}_{\beta+1}^{-d}\cdot\mathfrak{B}_{\beta}\cdot\mathfrak{B}_{\beta+1}^{d-1}

(350)

\textstyle=\sqrt{d}\frac{\mathfrak{B}_{\beta}}{\mathfrak{B}_{\beta+1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{d}\frac{4}{3},

(351)

where step (i) can be verified numerically.

Bounds on
R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}
, and
R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime}

Using the property of convolution, we find that
f_{\beta+1}(a)=0
if
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|a}}\right|\geq\frac{1}{2}(\beta+1)
.
Hence
{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(z)=0
for
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{\infty}>(\beta+1)/2
and applying the definitions
Definition

7
, we find that

\textstyle R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}\leq\sqrt{d}(\beta+1)/2\quad\text{and}\quad R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime}\leq\sqrt{d}(\beta+1)/2

(352)

as claimed in
Eq.

343
.

For completeness, we illustrate a key simplification that can readily yield the results stated in

Define
\zeta_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\mathcal{S}_{n}}

\textstyle\zeta_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\mathcal{S}_{n}}\triangleq\frac{1}{d}\max(R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\sqrt{n}}^{\prime},R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}})^{2},

(353)

where
R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}

7
and
7
respectively.
Then applying

in

\textstyle\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mathcal{S}_{n},\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{KT}})

\textstyle\!\leq\!2\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}\!+\!\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}(B\zeta_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\mathcal{S}_{n}})^{\frac{d}{4}}\!\cdot\!d^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\log\frac{n}{\delta}\!\cdot\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\log(\frac{1}{\delta})\!+\!d\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}+R)}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}}\right)}}\right]}

(354)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\precsim_{\delta}}}2\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}(B\zeta_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},\mathcal{S}_{n}})^{\frac{d}{4}}\cdot d^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}(R_{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}},n}+R_{\mathcal{S}_{n}})}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}}}}\right\|_{\infty}}}}\right)},

with probability at least
1-2\delta
, where

is a universal constant, and

r=\sqrt{\alpha d}

\textstyle c_{d}r^{\frac{d}{2}}=\frac{(4\pi)^{\frac{d}{4}}}{(\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1))^{\frac{1}{2}}}(\alpha d)^{\frac{d}{4}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\frac{(8e\pi\alpha)^{\frac{d}{4}}}{(\pi d)^{\frac{1}{4}}}\leq(B\alpha)^{\frac{d}{4}}d^{-\frac{1}{4}}\quad\text{where}\quad B\triangleq 8e\pi,

(356)

and step (ii) follows (for

. Now the results in

in

.

Appendix P
Supplementary Details for Vignettes of
Section

7

This section provides supplementary details for the vignettes of
Section

7
.

P.1
Mixture of Gaussians target

Our mixture of Gaussians target is given by

\mathbb{P}=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\mathcal{N}(\mu_{j},\mathbf{I}_{d})
for
M\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{4,6,8}}\right\}
where

\textstyle\mu_{1}

\textstyle=[-3,3]^{\top},\quad\mu_{2}=[-3,3]^{\top},\quad\mu_{3}=[-3,-3]^{\top},\quad\mu_{4}=[3,-3]^{\top},

(357)

\textstyle\mu_{5}

\textstyle=[0,6]^{\top},\qquad\mu_{6}=[-6,0]^{\top},\quad\mu_{7}=[6,0]^{\top},\qquad\mu_{8}=[0,-6]^{\top}.

(358)

P.2
Empirical decay rates

In
Fig.

5
, we provide results for regressing the true error rate of
\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
and
\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}
to the input size
n
(on the log-log scale as in all of mean MMD plots) for various ranges of coreset sizes. Notably, the observed empirical rates are significantly slower than
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for the smallest coreset range (the same coreset range used in the panels in
Figs.

3
and
3
) but approach
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}
as the coreset size increases.
Hence, the observed empirical rates of
Fig.

3
are consistent with
\frac{\log^{c}n}{\sqrt{n}}
rates of decay.

(a)

True error scaling of
\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

(b)

True error scaling of
\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}

Figure 5:
Empirical decay rates when true error
=\frac{\log^{c}n}{\sqrt{n}}
.
We display the ordinary least squares fits for regressing the log of true error onto the log of input size
n
when the true error equals either (a)
\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
or (b)
\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}
. As the input range increases, the fitted decay rate tends towards
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}
despite appearing significantly slower for smaller input ranges.

P.3
MCMC vignette details

For complete details on the targets and sampling algorithms we refer the reader to
Riabiz et al. 2021
.
When applying standard thinning to any Markov chain output, we adopt the convention of keeping the final sample point.
For all experiments, we used only the odd indices of the post burn-in sample points when thinning to form
\mathcal{S}_{n}
.

The selected burn-in periods for the Goodwin task were 820,000 for RW; 824,000 for adaRW; 1,615,000 for MALA; and 1,475,000 for pMALA. The respective numbers for the Lotka-Volterra task were 1,512,000 for RW; 1,797,000 for adaRW; 1,573,000 for MALA; and 1,251,000 for pMALA.
For the Hinch experiments, we discard the first
10^{6}
points as burn-in following
Riabiz et al. 2021
. For all
n
, the parameter
\sigma
for the Gaussian kernel is set to the median distance between all pairs of
4^{7}
points obtained by standard thinning the post-burn-in odd indices. The resulting values for the Goodwin chains were 0.02 for RW; 0.0201 for adaRW;
0.0171 for MALA; and 0.0205 for pMALA. The respective numbers for Lotka-Volterra task were 0.0274 for RW; 0.0283 for adaRW;
0.023 for MALA; and 0.0288 for pMALA. Finally, the numbers for the Hinch task were 8.0676 for RW; 8.3189 for adaRW;
8.621 for MALA; and 8.6649 for pMALA.

Appendix Q
Kernel Thinning with Square-root Dominating Kernels

As alluded to in
Section

2
, it is not necessary to identify an exact square-root kernel
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}

to run kernel thinning. Rather, it suffices to identify any
square-root dominating kernel

\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
defined as follows.

Definition 8

(Square-root dominating kernel)
.

We say a reproducing kernel
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is a
square-root dominating kernel
for a reproducing kernel
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
if
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,\cdot)
is square-integrable for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and either of the following equivalent conditions hold.

(a)

The RKHS of
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
belongs
to the RKHS of
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}(x,y)\triangleq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,z)\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(y,z)dz
.

(b)

The function
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}-c{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}

is positive definite for some
c>0
.

Remark 9

(Controlling
\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}
)
.

A square-root dominating kernel
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
is a suitable surrogate for
{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
as, by
Zhang and Zhao 2013
,
\mmd_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}(\mu,\nu)\leq\sqrt{1/c}\cdot\mmd_{\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}}(\mu,\nu)
for
c
the constant appearing in
Definition

8
(b)
and all distributions
\mu
and
\nu
.

Definitions

8
and
9
enable us to use convenient dominating surrogates whenever an exact square-root kernel is inconvenient to derive or deploy.
For example, our next result, proved in
Section

Q.1
, shows that a standard Matérn kernel is a square-root dominating kernel for every sufficiently-smooth shift-invariant and absolutely integrable
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
.

Proposition 4

(Dominating smooth kernels)
.

If
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,y)=\kappa(x-y)
and
\kappa\in{L}^{1}\cap C^{2\nu}
for
\nu>d
, then, for any
\gamma>0
, the
Matérn
(\frac{\nu}{2},\gamma)
kernel of
Table

1
is a square-root dominating kernel for
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
.

Checking the square-root dominating condition is also particularly simple for any pair of continuous shift-invariant kernels as the next result, proved in
Section

Q.2
, shows.

Proposition 5

(Dominating shift-invariant kernels)
.

Suppose that the real-valued kernels

{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,y)=\kappa(x-y)

and

\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)=\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x-y)

have respective spectral densities (
Definition

6
)

\widehat{\kappa}
and
{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
.
If
{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\in{L}^{2}
,
then

\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}

is a square-root dominating kernel for
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
if and only if

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{}}{ess\,sup}_{\omega\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\,\widehat{\kappa}(\omega)>0}\textstyle{\frac{\widehat{\kappa}(\omega)}{{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(\omega)^{2}}}<\infty.

(359)

In
Table

5
, we use
Proposition

5
to derive convenient tailored square-root dominating kernels
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
for standard inverse multiquadric kernels, hyberbolic secant (sech) kernels, and Wendland’s compactly supported kernels.
In each case, we can identify a square-root dominating kernel from the same family.

{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,y)=\kappa(x-y)
Name of kernel

\kappa(z)
Expression for

kernel

\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)=\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x-y)
Name of square-root dominating

\nu>0,\gamma>0
InverseMultiquadric
(\nu,\gamma)

(\gamma^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}^{2})^{-\nu}

; see
Eq.

373

(\nu^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})

(a)

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{d}\textstyle\sech\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}az_{j}}}\right)

Wendland

; see
Eq.

360

s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0},s^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{4}(2s\!-\!1\!-d)

Square-root dominating kernels
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
for common kernels
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
(see

). Here
\mathbb{N}_{0}
denotes the non-negative integers.

for our derivation.

Expressions for Wendland kernels

The
Wendland
(s)
kernel is a compactly-supported radial kernel
\phi_{d,s}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{x-y}}}\right\|_{2})
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
where
\phi_{d,s}:\real_{+}\to\real
is a truncated minimal-degree polynomial with
2s
continuous derivatives.
We collect here the expressions for
\phi_{d,s}
for
s=0,1,2
and refer the readers to
Wendland 2004
for more general
s
. Let
(r)_{+}=\max(0,r)
and
\ell\triangleq\lfloor{d/2}\rfloor+3
, then we have

\textstyle\phi_{d,0}(r)

\textstyle=(1-r)_{+}^{\lfloor{d/2}\rfloor+1},\quad\phi_{d,1}(r)=(1-r)_{+}^{\lfloor{d/2}\rfloor+2}[(\lfloor{d/2}\rfloor+2)r+1]

(360)

\textstyle\phi_{d,2}(r)

\textstyle=(1-r)_{+}^{\lfloor{d/2}\rfloor+3}[(\ell^{2}+4\ell+3)r^{2}+(3\ell+6)r+3].

(361)

Q.1
Proof of
Proposition

4
: Dominating smooth kernels

Since
\kappa\in{L}^{1}
,
\mathcal{F}(\kappa)
is bounded by the Babenko-Beckner inequality
(
Beckner 1975
)
and nonnegative by Bochner’s theorem
(
Bochner 1933
;
Wendland 2004
, Thm. 6.6)
.
Moreover, since
\kappa\in C^{2\nu}
,
Sun 1993
implies that
\int\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2\nu}\mathcal{F}(\kappa)(\omega)d\omega<\infty
.
By
Wendland 2004
,
the
Matérn
(\nu,\gamma)
kernel
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}(x,y)\propto\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}(x-y)
for
\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}
continuous with
\mathcal{F}(\Phi_{\nu,\gamma})(\omega)={}{(\gamma^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2})^{-\nu}}
.
Since we have established that

\textstyle\int\frac{\mathcal{F}(\kappa)(\omega)^{2}}{\mathcal{F}(\Phi_{\nu,\gamma})(\omega)}d\omega\!=\!\int(\gamma^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2})^{\nu}\mathcal{F}(\kappa)(\omega)^{2}d\omega\!\leq\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathcal{F}(\kappa)}}}\right\|_{\infty}\int(\gamma^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2})^{\nu}\mathcal{F}(\kappa)(\omega)d\omega\!<\!\infty,

(362)

Wendland 2004
implies that
\kappa
belongs to
\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}}
and hence that
\mathcal{H}_{{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}}
.
Finally, by
Section

N.1
,
Matérn
(\frac{\nu}{2},\gamma)
is a valid square-root dominating kernel for
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}
and therefore for
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
.

Q.2
Proof of
Proposition

5
: Dominating shift-invariant kernels

Definition

6
implies that

\textstyle{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x,y)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/2}}\int e^{-i\langle{\omega},{x-y}\rangle}\widehat{\kappa}(\omega)d\omega\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,y)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/2}}\int e^{-i\langle{\omega},{x-y}\rangle}{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(\omega)d\omega,

(363)

Moreover, since

{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,\cdot)=\mathcal{F}(e^{-i\langle{\cdot},{x}\rangle}{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}})
for

e^{-i\langle{\cdot},{x}\rangle}{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\in{L}^{1}\cap{L}^{2}
, the Plancherel-Parseval identity
(
Wendland 2004
, Proof of Thm. 5.23)
implies that

\textstyle\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}(x,y)\triangleq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(x,z)\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(y,z)dz

\textstyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-i\langle{\omega},{x}\rangle}{{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(\omega)}\ e^{i\langle{\omega},{y}\rangle}{{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(\omega)}d\omega

(364)

\textstyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-i\langle{\omega},{x-y}\rangle}{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(\omega)^{2}d\omega,

(365)

and Bochner’s theorem
(
Bochner 1933
;
Wendland 2004
, Thm. 6.6)
implies that
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}
is a kernel.
Finally, Prop. 3.1 of
Zhang and Zhao 2013
now implies that the RKHS of
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}
belongs to the RKHS of
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}
if and only if the ratio condition
Eq.

359
holds.

Q.3
Derivation of
Table

5
: Square-root dominating kernels
\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
for common kernels
{\mathbf{k}_{\star}}

Thanks to
Proposition

5
, to establish the validity of the square root dominating kernels stated in
Table

5
, it suffices to verify that

\textstyle{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\in{L}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\kappa}\precsim_{\,d,\kappa,\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}}{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}^{2},

(366)

where the functions
\kappa
and
{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}
are the spectral densities of
\kappa
and
\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}
.

Inverse multiquadric

Consider the positive definite function
\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}

Eq.

260
underlying the Matérn kernel, which is continuous on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{{\boldsymbol{0}}\}
.
When
\kappa(z)=(\gamma^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}^{2})^{-\nu}

and

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(z)=({\gamma^{\prime}}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2}^{2})^{-\nu^{\prime}}
,

Wendland 2004
implies that

\textstyle\widehat{\kappa}(\omega)=\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}(\omega)\quad\text{and}\quad\Phi_{\nu^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}^{2}(\omega)={\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(\omega)^{2}.

(367)

Let
a(\omega)\asymp_{d,\nu,\gamma}b(\omega)
denote asymptotic equivalence up to a constant depending on
d,\nu,\gamma
.
Then, by
(
DLMF
, Eqs. 10.25.3 & 10.30.2)
,
we have

\textstyle\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}(\omega)

\textstyle\asymp_{d,\nu,\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{\nu-\frac{d}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}}\quad\text{as}\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}\to\infty,

(368)

\textstyle\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}(\omega)

\textstyle\asymp_{d,\nu,\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{-(d-2\nu)_{+}}\quad\text{as}\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}\to 0.

(369)

Hence,
{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}\in{L}^{2}
whenever
\nu^{\prime}>\frac{d}{4}
.

Moreover, applying
Eq.

368
, we find that for
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}\to\infty
,

\textstyle\frac{\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}(\omega)}{\Phi_{\nu^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}^{2}(\omega)}

\textstyle\asymp_{d,\nu,\gamma,\nu^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{\nu-\frac{d}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{-2\nu^{\prime}+d+1}e^{2\gamma^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}}

(370)

\textstyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}+\frac{1}{2}-2\nu^{\prime}}e^{(2\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}}.

(371)

If
2\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma<0
, this expression is bounded for any value of
\nu^{\prime}
.
If
2\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma=0
, this expression is bounded when
\nu^{\prime}\geq\frac{\nu}{2}+\frac{d}{4}+\frac{1}{4}
.

Applying
Eq.

369
, we find that for
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}\to 0
,

\textstyle\frac{\Phi_{\nu,\gamma}(\omega)}{\Phi_{\nu^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}^{2}(\omega)}

\textstyle\asymp_{d,\nu,\gamma,\nu^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{-(d-2\nu)_{+}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2(d-2\nu^{\prime})_{+}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2(d-2\nu^{\prime})_{+}-(d-2\nu)_{+}},

(372)

If
\nu\geq\frac{d}{2}
, this expression is finite for any value of
\nu^{\prime}
.
If
\nu<\frac{d}{2}
, this expression is finite when
\nu^{\prime}\leq\frac{\nu}{2}+\frac{d}{4}
.
Hence, our condition
Eq.

366
is verified whenever
(\nu^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})
belongs to the set

\textstyle\mathcal{C}_{\nu,\gamma,d}\triangleq\big\{(\nu^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}):\

\textstyle(1)\ \nu^{\prime}>\frac{d}{4}\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma^{\prime}\leq\frac{\gamma}{2},\quad\text{and}\quad

(373)

\textstyle(2)\ \nu^{\prime}\leq\frac{d}{4}+\frac{\nu}{2}\quad\text{if}\quad\nu<\frac{d}{2},\quad\text{and}\quad

(374)

\textstyle(3)\ \nu^{\prime}\geq\frac{d}{4}+\frac{\nu}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma^{\prime}=\frac{\gamma}{2}\big\}.

(375)

Sech

Define
\kappa_{a}(z)\triangleq\prod_{j=1}^{d}\sech\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}az_{j}}}\right)
, and suppose
\kappa=\kappa_{a}
and
\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}=\kappa_{2a}
.

Huggins and Mackey 2018
yields that

\textstyle\widehat{\kappa}(\omega)=\mathcal{F}(\kappa_{a})(\omega)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{a^{d}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}\sech(\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{\omega_{j}}{a})=a^{-d}\cdot\kappa_{1/a}(\omega),\quad\text{and}\quad

(376)

\textstyle{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(\omega)=\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}})(\omega)

\textstyle=(2a)^{-d}\kappa_{1/(2a)}(\omega).

(377)

Since
\sech^{2}(b/2)=\frac{4}{e^{b}+e^{-b}+2}>\frac{4}{e^{b}+e^{-b}}=2\sech(b)
, we have

\textstyle\kappa_{1/a}(\omega)\leq 2^{-d}\cdot\kappa_{1/(2a)}(\omega)^{2}.

(378)

Putting the pieces together, we further have

\textstyle\widehat{\kappa}(\omega)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:sech_fourier}}}{{=}}a^{-d}\cdot\kappa_{1/a}(\omega)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:sech_bound_kappa}}}{{\leq}}(2a)^{-d}\cdot\kappa_{1/(2a)}(\omega)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:sech_fourier_sqrt}}}{{=}}(2a)^{d}\cdot{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(\omega)^{2}.

(379)

Since
\kappa_{1/(2a)}\in{L}^{2}
, we have verified the condition
Eq.

366
.

Wendland

When
\kappa(z)=\phi_{d,s}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2})

and

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathrm{rt}}(z)=\phi_{d,s^{\prime}}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{z}}}\right\|_{2})
,

Wendland 2004
implies that

\textstyle\widehat{\kappa}(\omega)\precsim_{d,s,s^{\prime}}\frac{1}{(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2})^{d+2s+1}}\leq\frac{1}{(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2})^{2d+4s^{\prime}+2}}\precsim_{d,s,s^{\prime}}{\widehat{\kappa}}_{\mathrm{rt}}(\omega)^{2}\precsim_{d,s,s^{\prime}}\frac{1}{(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\omega}}}\right\|_{2}^{2})^{2d+4s^{\prime}+2}},

(380)

with
s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0},s^{\prime}\leq\frac{(2s\!-\!1\!-d)}{4}
, thereby establishing the condition
Eq.

366
.

Appendix R
Online Vector Balancing in Euclidean Space

Using
Theorem

3
, we recover the online vector balancing result of
Alweiss et al. 2021
with improved constants and a less conservative setting of the thresholds
\mathfrak{a}_{i}
.
Note that we capture the usual obliviousness assumption by treating the sequence
(f_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
as a fixed, deterministic input to
Algorithm

5
.

Corollary 7

(Online vector balancing in Euclidean space)
.

If
\mathcal{H}=\mathbb{R}^{d}
equipped with the Euclidean dot product, each
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{f_{i}}}}\right\|_{2}\leq 1
, and each
\mathfrak{a}_{i}=\frac{1}{2}+\log(4n/\delta)
, then,
with probability at least
1-\delta
,
the self-balancing Hilbert walk (
Algorithm

5
) returns
\psi_{n}
satisfying

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\psi_{n}}}}\right\|_{\infty}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}f_{i}}}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{2\log(4d/\delta)\log(4n/\delta)}.

(381)

Proof

Instantiate the notation of
Theorem

3
.
With our choice of
\mathfrak{a}_{i}
,
the signed sum representation property
Item

(ii)
implies that
\psi_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}f_{i}
with probability at least
1-\delta/2
.
Moreover, the union bound, the functional sub-Gaussianity property
Item

(i)
, and
the sub-Gaussian Hoeffding inequality
(
Wainwright 2019
, Prop. 2.5)
now imply

\textstyle\mathrm{Pr}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\psi_{n}}}}\right\|_{\infty}>t\mid\mathcal{F}_{n})\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{P}(|\langle{\psi_{n}},{e_{j}}\rangle|>t\mid\mathcal{F}_{n})

\textstyle\leq 2d\exp(-t^{2}/(2\sigma_{n}^{2}))=\delta/2

(382)

\textstyle\quad\text{for}\quad t

\textstyle\triangleq\sigma_{n}\sqrt{2\log(4d/\delta)}.

(383)

Since
\mathfrak{a}_{i}
is non-decreasing in
i
,
and
{\textstyle\frac{\frac{1}{2}+\log(4/\delta)}{\log(4/\delta)}}
is increasing in
\delta\in(0,1]
,

\textstyle\sigma_{n}^{2}

\textstyle=\max_{j\leq n}{\textstyle\frac{\mathfrak{a}_{j}^{2}}{2\mathfrak{a}_{j}-\|{f_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}}\leq\max_{j\leq n}{\textstyle\frac{\mathfrak{a}_{j}^{2}}{2\mathfrak{a}_{j}-1}}=\log(4n/\delta){\textstyle\frac{(\frac{1}{2}+\log(4n/\delta))^{2}}{2(\log(4n/\delta))^{2}}}

(384)

\textstyle\leq\log(4n/\delta){\textstyle\frac{(\frac{1}{2}+\log(4))^{2}}{2(\log(4))^{2}}}\leq\log(4n/\delta).

(385)

The advertised result now follows from the union bound.

Appendix S
L^{\infty}
Coresets of
Phillips and Tai 2020
and
Tai 2020

Here we provide more details on the
L^{\infty}
coreset construction of
Phillips and Tai 2020
and
Tai 2020
discussed in
Section

8.3
.
Given input points
(x_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
, the Phillips-Tai (PT) construction forms the matrix
K=({\mathbf{k}_{\star}}(x_{i},x_{j}))_{i,j=1}^{n}
of pairwise kernel evaluations, finds a matrix square-root
V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
satisfying
K=VV^{\top}
, and augments
V
with a row of ones (to encourage near-halving in the next step). Then, the PT construction runs the Gram-Schmidt (GS) walk of
Bansal et al. 2018
to identify approximately half of the columns of
V
as coreset members and
rebalances
the coreset until exactly half of the input points belong to the coreset.
The GS walk and rebalancing steps are recursively repeated
\Omega(\log(n))
times to obtain an
(n^{\frac{1}{2}},\mathcal{O}_{p}(\sqrt{d}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\log n}))
-
L^{\infty}
coreset.
The low-dimensional Gaussian kernel construction of
Tai 2020
first partitions the input points into balls of radius
2\sqrt{\log n}
and then applies the PT construction separately to each ball.
The result is an

(n^{\frac{1}{2}},\mathcal{O}_{p}(2^{d}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\log(d\log n)}))
-
L^{\infty}
coreset with an additional superexponential
\Omega(d^{5d})
running time dependence.
